\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{(\mathbf{N})}\bigg\{\Gamma_{N}\leq\frac{C_{10}\gamma}{\sqrt{N}}\cdot\sum_{s,a}\sqrt{b_{\mu}(s,a)}\cdot\bigg(\int_{0}^{\infty}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{N}(\Theta,\mathbb{W}_{1,\infty},t)}\mathrm{d}t+u\cdot{\rm diam}(\Theta;\mathbb{W}_{1,\infty})\bigg)

\displaystyle+2\epsilon_{1}+\frac{1}{N}\cdot\frac{8}{p_{{\rm min}}}\cdot\bigg(\sup_{s,a}\mathbb{E}\|R(s,a)\|+\gamma\cdot\sup_{s,a}\mathbb{E}\|Z(s,a;\theta_{0})\|\bigg)\bigg\}

\displaystyle\geq 1-2\exp(-u^{2})-(2|\mathcal{S\times A}|+6N)\cdot\exp\bigg\{\frac{-C_{21}\cdot p_{{\rm min}}\cdot N\cdot\epsilon_{1}^{2}}{d^{2}\cdot\big(\sup_{s,a}\|R(s,a)\|_{\psi_{2}}\big)^{2}+\gamma^{2}\cdot\big(\sup_{s,a}\mathbb{E}\|Z(s,a;\theta_{0})\|\big)^{2}}\bigg\},

(37)

where the second last inequality holds by Inequality (
35
) and the technique
(a+b)^{2}\leq 2(a^{2}+b^{2})
. We should always remember that we are conditioning on the event
\Omega_{\mathcal{S\times A}}^{(\epsilon)}
defined in Definition (
29
).

A.7.4
Bounding State-action Discrepancy

This time, we will bound state-action discrepancy
\Delta_{N}
that occurs due to the estimation error of
b_{\mu}(s,a)
. As warned in the last paragraph of
A.7.2
, we are still assuming
N(s,a)
to be fixed, satisfying Facts (
30
). Accordingly, we only deal with Stage 2 probability space (
28
) with the conditional probability measure
\mathbb{P}^{(\mathbf{N})}
.

With
\mathbf{p}
and
\hat{\mathbf{p}}
defined in
A.7.2
and
\epsilon>0
being the value specified in Definition (
29
), and defining
\|\mathbf{x}\|_{1}:=\sum_{i=1}^{p_{0}}|x_{i}|
for
\forall\mathbf{x}=(x_{1},\cdots,x_{p_{0}})^{\intercal}
, we can derive the following based on
(x_{1}+\cdots+x_{p_{0}})^{2}\leq p_{0}\cdot(x_{1}^{2}+\cdots x_{p_{0}}^{2})
,

\displaystyle\sum_{s,a}\bigg|\hat{b}_{\mu}(s,a)-b_{\mu}(s,a)\bigg|=\|\mathbf{p}-\hat{\mathbf{p}}\|_{1}\leq\sqrt{|\mathcal{S\times A}|}\cdot\|\mathbf{p}-\hat{\mathbf{p}}\|\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{p_{\rm min}}}\cdot\|\mathbf{p}-\hat{\mathbf{p}}\|,

where we used
p_{\rm min}\leq 1/|\mathcal{S\times A}|
. Then we have the following extension for
\Delta_{N}
defined in Section
A.7.1
,

\displaystyle\Delta_{N}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sqrt{p_{{\rm min}}}}{2}\cdot\epsilon\cdot\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\sup_{s,a}\mathcal{E}\bigg\{\Upsilon_{\theta}(s,a),\hat{\mathcal{T}}^{\pi}\Upsilon_{\theta}(s,a)\bigg\}\ \ \text{by Facts (\ref{Fact1to3})}.

Now let us handle the supremum term. Letting
R_{i}(s,a)\ (1\leq i\leq N(s,a))
be the reward vectors observed conditioned on
s,a
, we have the following hold based on the notations introduced in (
11
),

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\sup_{s,a}\mathcal{E}\bigg\{\Upsilon_{\theta}(s,a),\hat{\mathcal{T}}^{\pi}\Upsilon_{\theta}(s,a)\bigg\}\leq 4(1+\gamma)\cdot\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\sup_{s,a}\mathbb{E}\|Z(s,a;\theta)\|+4\cdot\sup_{s,a}\bigg\{\frac{1}{N(s,a)}\cdot\sum_{i=1}^{N(s,a)}\|R_{i}(s,a)\|\bigg\}.

The first term can be bounded as follows, using the property of
\mathbb{W}_{1,\infty}
introduced in Appendix
A.6
,

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\sup_{s,a}\mathbb{E}\|Z(s,a;\theta)\|=\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\bigg\{\sup_{s,a}\mathbb{E}\|Z(s,a;\theta)\|-\sup_{s,a}\mathbb{E}\|Z(s,a;\theta_{0})\|\bigg\}+\sup_{s,a}\mathbb{E}\|Z(s,a;\theta_{0})\|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\mathbb{W}_{1,\infty}(\theta,\theta_{0})+\sup_{s,a}\mathbb{E}\|Z(s,a;\theta_{0})\|\leq{\rm diam}(\Theta;\mathbb{W}_{1,\infty})+\sup_{s,a}\mathbb{E}\|Z(s,a;\theta_{0})\|.

Now let
\epsilon_{2}>0
be arbitrary. For a fixed
s,a\in\mathcal{S\times A}
, we can derive the following based on previous techniques and (
35
),

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{(\mathbf{N})}\bigg[\sup_{s,a}\bigg\{

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N(s,a)}\sum_{i=1}^{N(s,a)}\|R_{i}(s,a)\|\bigg\}\geq\sup_{s,a}\mathbb{E}\|R(s,a)\|+\epsilon_{2}\bigg]\leq 2|\mathcal{S\times A}|\cdot\exp\bigg\{\frac{-C_{23}\cdot p_{{\rm min}}\cdot N\cdot\epsilon_{2}^{2}}{d^{2}\cdot\big(\sup_{s,a}\|R(s,a)\|_{\psi_{2}}\big)^{2}}\bigg\}.

(38)

Then this gives us

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{(\mathbf{N})}\bigg[\Delta_{N}\leq 2\sqrt{p_{{\rm min}}}\cdot\epsilon\cdot\bigg\{(1+\gamma)\cdot\bigg({\rm diam}(\Theta;\mathbb{W}_{1,\infty})+\sup_{s,a}\mathbb{E}\|Z(s,a;\theta_{0})\|\bigg)

\displaystyle+\sup_{s,a}\mathbb{E}\|R(s,a)\|+\epsilon_{2}\bigg\}\bigg]\geq 1-2|\mathcal{S\times A}|\cdot\exp\bigg\{\frac{-C_{23}\cdot p_{{\rm min}}\cdot N\cdot\epsilon_{2}^{2}}{d^{2}\cdot\big(\sup_{s,a}\|R(s,a)\|_{\psi_{2}}\big)^{2}}\bigg\}.

(39)

A.7.5
Finalizing The Bound

Recall that we defined
\Omega_{\mathcal{S\times A}}^{(\epsilon)}
(
29
) in
A.7.2
where the samples are collected sufficiently many for each
s,a
. Assuming this, we have bounded
\Gamma_{N}
and
\Delta_{N}
throughout
A.7.3
and
A.7.4
, each in (
37
) and (
39
). Simply put, letting
E\subset\Omega
be the event where
\Gamma_{N}
and
\Delta_{N}
simultaneously achieve the specified bounds (
37
) and (
39
) can be understood as
\mathbb{P}(E|\Omega_{\mathcal{S\times A}}^{(\epsilon)})
. Skipping detailed derivations, we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(E)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}(E\cap\Omega_{\mathcal{S\times A}}^{(\epsilon)})\geq 1-(1-\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{\mathcal{S\times A}}^{(\epsilon)}))-(1-\mathbb{P}(E|\Omega_{\mathcal{S\times A}}^{(\epsilon)})).

(40)

According to (
28
), it may be more rigorous to denote
\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{S\times A}}(\Omega_{\mathcal{S\times A}}^{(\epsilon)})
instead of
\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{\mathcal{S\times A}}^{(\epsilon)})
in (
40
), but we allowed using
\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{\mathcal{S\times A}}^{(\epsilon)})
since
\mathbb{P}
is an integrated probability measure of both
\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{S\times A}}
and
\mathbb{P}^{(\mathbf{N})}
.

Term
\mathbb{P}(E|\Omega_{\mathcal{S\times A}}^{(\epsilon)})
can be derived by aggregating two bounds (
37
) and (
39
). That is, for
\forall\epsilon_{1}>0,\ \epsilon_{2}>0,\ u>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(E|\Omega_{\mathcal{S\times A}}^{(\epsilon)})

\displaystyle\geq 1-2|\mathcal{S\times A}|\cdot\exp\bigg\{\frac{-C_{23}\cdot p_{{\rm min}}\cdot N\cdot\epsilon_{2}^{2}}{d^{2}\cdot\big(\sup_{s,a}\|R(s,a)\|_{\psi_{2}}\big)^{2}}\bigg\}-2\exp(-u^{2})

\displaystyle-(2|\mathcal{S\times A}|+6N)\cdot\exp\bigg\{\frac{-C_{21}\cdot p_{{\rm min}}\cdot N\cdot\epsilon_{1}^{2}}{d^{2}\cdot\big(\sup_{s,a}\|R(s,a)\|_{\psi_{2}}\big)^{2}+\gamma^{2}\cdot\big(\sup_{s,a}\mathbb{E}\|Z(s,a;\theta_{0})\|\big)^{2}}\bigg\}.

Then it remains for us to calculate
\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{\mathcal{S\times A}}^{(\epsilon)})
in (
40
), and we should assume
\epsilon\in(0,1]
as mentioned in Definition (
29
). Towards that end, we use the following lemma that we proved in
C.1.5
,

Lemma A.3
.

For
\mathbf{X}_{i}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\sim}\limits^{iid}}{\rm Multinomial}(n,\mathbf{p})
with
\mathbf{p}=(p_{1},\cdots,p_{H})^{\intercal}
with
\sum_{h=1}^{H}p_{h}=1
, we have the following for
\forall\epsilon>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(\|\hat{\mathbf{p}}-\mathbf{p}\|\geq\epsilon\big)\leq\exp\bigg(\frac{1}{4}\bigg)\cdot\exp\bigg(\frac{-n\cdot\epsilon^{2}}{32}\bigg)\ \ \text{where }\ \hat{\mathbf{p}}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{X}_{i}.

If
N\geq 2
holds (as we assumed in
A.7.2
), we have
\lfloor N/2\rfloor\geq N/6
and
(N-\lfloor N/2\rfloor)\geq N/6
. Then applying the above lemma leads to

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{\mathcal{S\times A}}^{(\epsilon)})

\displaystyle\geq 1-C_{24}\cdot\exp(-C_{25}\cdot p_{{\rm min}}^{2}\cdot N\cdot\epsilon^{2}).

(41)

We adjust the existing variables as
\epsilon_{1}=\sqrt{p_{{\rm min}}}\cdot\epsilon,\ \epsilon_{2}=\sqrt{p_{{\rm min}}}\cdot\epsilon,\ u=\sqrt{N}\cdot p_{{\rm min}}\cdot\epsilon
.
Based on following, which holds due to Cauchy-Schwartz Inequality,

\displaystyle\sum_{s,a}\sqrt{b_{\mu}(s,a)}\leq\bigg(\sum_{s,a}b_{\mu}(s,a)\bigg)^{1/2}\cdot\bigg(\sum_{s,a}1\bigg)^{1/2}=\sqrt{|\mathcal{S\times A}|}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{p_{{\rm min}}}},

(42)

we can rewrite the probability bound as follows, skipping technical details. With probability larger than
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\bar{\mathcal{E}}(\Upsilon_{\hat{\theta}},\Upsilon_{\pi})\leq\frac{32C_{29}}{\sqrt{p_{{\rm min}}}}\cdot C_{{\rm sup}}\cdot B_{1}(\gamma)\cdot C_{\rm env}(\Theta)\cdot\sqrt{\frac{1}{N}\log\bigg(\frac{C_{27}\cdot(|\mathcal{S\times A}|+N)}{\delta}\bigg)},

(43)

under
N\geq N_{(1)}(\delta)
and
N\geq N_{(2)}(\delta)
.
N\geq N_{(1)}(\delta)
implies

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\log\bigg(\frac{C_{27}\cdot(|\mathcal{S\times A}|+N)}{\delta}\bigg)\leq\frac{C_{28}\cdot p_{{\rm min}}^{2}}{C_{\rm den}}.

N\geq N_{(2)}(\delta)
implies

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\cdot\frac{8}{p_{{\rm min}}}\cdot\bigg(\sup_{s,a}\mathbb{E}\|R(s,a)\|+\gamma\cdot\sup_{s,a}\mathbb{E}\|Z(s,a;\theta_{0})\|\bigg)+\frac{C_{10}\gamma}{\sqrt{N}}\cdot\sqrt{|\mathcal{S\times A}|}

\displaystyle\times\int_{0}^{\infty}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{N}(\Theta,\mathbb{W}_{1,\infty},t)}\mathrm{d}t\leq\frac{C_{29}\cdot(1+\gamma)}{\sqrt{p_{{\rm min}}}}\cdot C_{\rm env}(\Theta)\cdot\sqrt{\frac{1}{N}\log\bigg(\frac{C_{27}\cdot(|\mathcal{S\times A}|+N)}{\delta}\bigg)}.

Here are definitions of terms.

\displaystyle C_{\rm den}:=d^{2}\cdot\big(\sup_{s,a}\|R(s,a)\|_{\psi_{2}}\big)^{2}+\gamma^{2}\cdot\big(\sup_{s,a}\mathbb{E}\|Z(s,a;\theta_{0})\|\big)^{2}+1,\quad\theta_{0}:\in\arg\min_{\theta\in\Theta}\sup_{s,a}\mathbb{E}\|Z(s,a;\theta)\|,

(44)

\displaystyle C_{{\rm env}}(\Theta):={\rm diam}(\Theta;\mathbb{W}_{1,\infty})^{2}+\big(\sup_{s,a}\mathbb{E}\|Z(s,a;\theta_{0})\|\big)^{2}+d^{2}\cdot\big(\sup_{s,a}\|R(s,a)\|_{\psi_{2}}\big)^{2}+1.

A.7.6
Under Slight Extent Of Non-realizability

As we have warned in Section
3.2
, having perfect realizability may be difficult in real practice. Therefore, we will slightly adjust the proof, in order to analyze the error bound of our estimator (
13
) under non-realizability.

We can take up from (
26
) as follows,

\displaystyle\bar{\mathcal{E}}(\Upsilon_{\hat{\theta}},\mathcal{T}^{\pi}\Upsilon_{\hat{\theta}})\leq 2\cdot\bigg\{2\cdot\bar{\mathcal{E}}(\Upsilon_{\theta_{*}},\mathcal{T}^{\pi}\Upsilon_{\theta_{*}})+2\cdot\bar{\mathcal{E}}(\mathcal{T}^{\pi}\Upsilon_{\theta_{*}},\hat{\mathcal{T}}^{\pi}\Upsilon_{\theta_{*}})+2\Delta_{N}+\Gamma_{N}\bigg\}

\displaystyle=4\cdot\bar{\mathcal{E}}(\Upsilon_{\theta_{*}},\mathcal{T}^{\pi}\Upsilon_{\theta_{*}})+4\Delta_{N}+6\Gamma_{N}.

Then, we can repeat the subsequent proofs of bounding
\Delta_{N}
and
\Gamma_{N}
, and thereby obtain the following with probability larger than
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\bar{\mathcal{E}}(\Upsilon_{\hat{\theta}},\Upsilon_{\pi})\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{p_{{\rm min}}}}\cdot C_{{\rm sup}}\cdot B_{1}(\gamma)\cdot C_{{\rm env}}(\Theta)\cdot\bigg\{\underbrace{\inf_{\theta\in\Theta}\bar{\mathcal{E}}(\Upsilon_{\theta},\mathcal{T}^{\pi}\Upsilon_{\theta})}_{\text{due to non-realizability}}+\sqrt{\frac{1}{N}\log\bigg(\frac{C_{2}\cdot(|\mathcal{S\times A}|+N)}{\delta}\bigg)}\bigg\}.

If the term
\inf_{\theta\in\Theta}\bar{\mathcal{E}}(\Upsilon_{\theta},\mathcal{T}^{\pi}\Upsilon_{\theta})
is very small, then the inaccuracy bound of
\bar{\mathcal{E}}(\Upsilon_{\hat{\theta}},\Upsilon_{\pi})
has only a small value that does not vanish as sample size grows
N\rightarrow\infty
.

A.8
Special Case For Parametric Models Under Realizability

In the case of
\Theta\subset\mathbb{R}^{p}
, by further assuming Assumption
4.1
, we can simplify the metric entropy term
\int_{0}^{\infty}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{N}(\Theta,\mathbb{W}_{1,\infty},t)}\mathrm{d}t
in (
A.7.5
), leading to the following corollary. If another metric
\tilde{\eta}
is used instead of
\mathbb{W}_{1,\infty}
, then Assumption
4.1
should be modified accordingly.
See Appendix
B.2
.

Corollary A.4
.

(Inaccuracy for parametric model in realizable scenario)
Under Assumptions
2.1
,
3.1
,
3.2
,
4.1
, for arbitrary
\delta\in(0,1)
, given large enough sample size
N\geq N(\delta):=\max\{2,N_{(1)}(\delta),N_{(2)}(\delta)\}
(defined before (
44
)), we have the following with probability larger than
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\bar{\mathcal{E}}(\Upsilon_{\hat{\theta}},\Upsilon_{\pi})\leq\frac{C_{1}}{\sqrt{p_{{\rm min}}}}\cdot C_{{\rm sup}}\cdot B_{1}(\gamma)\cdot C_{{\rm env}}^{\prime}(\Theta)\cdot\sqrt{\frac{1}{N}\log\bigg(\frac{C_{2}\cdot(|\mathcal{S\times A}|+N)}{\delta}\bigg)},

where
B_{1}(\gamma)>0
,
C_{{\rm sup}}>0
,
C_{\rm env}(\Theta)^{\prime}
are defined in Equations (
27
), (
19
) and (
45
), and
p_{{\rm min}}>0
under Assumption
2.1
.

A.8.1
Proof

We inherit the result of Appendix
A.7.5
, except several changes. Applying Assumption
4.1
, we have
{\rm diam}(\Theta;\mathbb{W}_{1,\infty})\leq L\cdot{\rm diam}(\Theta;\|\cdot\|)
, which allows us to replace
C_{\rm env}(\Theta)
with

\displaystyle C_{\rm env}^{\prime}(\Theta):=L^{2}\cdot{\rm diam}(\Theta;\|\cdot\|)^{2}+\big(\sup_{s,a}\mathbb{E}\|Z(s,a;\theta_{0})\|\big)^{2}+d^{2}\cdot\big(\sup_{s,a}\|R(s,a)\|_{\psi_{2}}\big)^{2}+1

(45)

where
{\rm diam}(\Theta;\|\cdot\|)<\infty
is ensured by compactness (Assumption
4.1
). In addition, we can make use of the following fact (proof in
C.1.6
) to further simplify
N_{(2)}(\delta)
based on following lemma.

Remark A.5
.

Under Assumption
4.1
, we have the following,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{N}(\Theta,\mathbb{W}_{1,\infty},t)}\mathrm{d}t\leq 6\sqrt{2\pi}\cdot L\sqrt{p}\cdot{\rm diam}(\Theta;\|\cdot\|).

A.9
Continuous State-action Space With Deterministic Transition

A.9.1
Estimator And Its Convergence

Assume the case where
\mathcal{S\times A}
is continuous (more generally speaking, there is zero probability of observing the same state-action pair twice). However, the transition
p(r,s^{\prime}|s,a)
is deterministic. That is, given
s,a
, there is no randomness in
(r,s^{\prime})
. In this case, our estimator (
12
) can achieve a
1/\sqrt{N}
convergence rate. Note that our estimator (
12
) becomes the following:

\displaystyle X_{\theta}:=2X_{\theta}^{(1)}-X_{\theta}^{(2)}-X_{\theta}^{(3)}\quad\&\quad\hat{\theta}:=\arg\min_{\theta\in\Theta}X_{\theta},

(46)

where the realizations of
X_{\theta}^{(1)}
,
X_{\theta}^{(2)}
,
X_{\theta}^{(3)}
based on observed data
\mathcal{D}=\{(s_{i},a_{i},r_{i},s_{i}^{\prime})\}_{i=1}^{N}
are as follows,

\displaystyle x_{\theta}^{(1)}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\|Z_{\alpha}(s_{i},a_{i};\theta)-r_{i}-\gamma Z_{\beta}(s_{i}^{\prime},A_{i}^{\prime};\theta)\|\quad\text{with }A_{i}^{\prime}\sim\pi(\cdot|s_{i}^{\prime}),

\displaystyle x_{\theta}^{(2)}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\|Z_{\alpha}(s_{i},a_{i};\theta)-Z_{\beta}(s_{i},a_{i};\theta)\|

\displaystyle x_{\theta}^{(3)}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\|Z_{\alpha}(s_{i}^{\prime},A_{i}^{\alpha};\theta)-Z_{\beta}(s_{i}^{\prime},A_{i}^{\beta};\theta)\|\quad\text{with }A_{i}^{\alpha},A_{i}^{\beta}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\sim}\limits^{iid}}\pi(\cdot|s_{i}^{\prime}),

where
\alpha
and
\beta
are used to indicate mutually independent distributions like we did in (
11
). We have
O_{p}(1/\sqrt{N})
convergence as follows.

Assumption A.6
.

We have
\sup_{t\in\mathbb{N}}\sup_{s,a}\{q_{b_{\mu}}^{\pi:t}(s,a)/b_{\mu}(s,a)\}\leq C_{{\rm sup}}<\infty
, where
q_{b_{\mu}}^{\pi:t}(s,a)
is defined in Section
A.1
.

Theorem A.7
.

Under Assumptions
A.6
,
3.2
, with probability larger than
1-\delta
, we have the following bounds.

For realizabilty (under Assumption
3.1
), we have

\displaystyle\bar{\mathcal{E}}(\Upsilon_{\hat{\theta}},\Upsilon_{\pi})\leq\frac{C\cdot C_{{\rm sup}}}{\sqrt{N}}\cdot B_{1}(\gamma;1,1)\cdot\bigg\{\int_{0}^{\infty}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{N}(\Theta,\mathbb{W}_{1,\infty},t)}\mathrm{d}t+C_{mod}\cdot\{{\rm diam}(\Theta;\mathbb{W}_{1,\infty})+1\}\cdot\sqrt{\log\bigg(\frac{12}{\delta}\bigg)}\bigg\}.

For non-realizabilty (without Assumption
3.1
), we have

\displaystyle\bar{\mathcal{E}}(\Upsilon_{\hat{\theta}},\Upsilon_{\pi})\leq C_{{\rm sup}}B_{1}(\gamma;1,1)\cdot\inf_{\theta\in\Theta}\bar{\mathcal{E}}(\Upsilon_{\theta_{*}},\mathcal{T}^{\pi}\Upsilon_{\theta_{*}})+\frac{C}{\sqrt{N}}\cdot C_{{\rm sup}}\cdot B_{1}(\gamma;1,1)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\bigg\{\int_{0}^{\infty}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{N}(\Theta,\mathbb{W}_{1,\infty},t)}\mathrm{d}t+C_{mod}\cdot\{{\rm diam}(\Theta;\mathbb{W}_{1,\infty})+1\}\cdot\sqrt{\log\bigg(\frac{12}{\delta}\bigg)}\bigg\}.

with
C_{mod}:=d\cdot\sup_{s,a}\|R(s,a)\|_{\psi_{2}}+\sup_{s,a}\mathbb{E}\|Z(s,a;\theta_{0})\|+1
and
B_{1}(\gamma;1,1):=\frac{1}{(1-\gamma)}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}(4^{k}\cdot\gamma^{(2^{k-1}-1)})
. The rate of
B_{1}(\gamma;1,1)
with respect to
\gamma\in(0,1)
is explained in (
22
).

If the realizability is satisfied, then we have
\inf_{\theta\in\Theta}\bar{\mathcal{E}}(\Upsilon_{\theta_{*}},\mathcal{T}^{\pi}\Upsilon_{\theta_{*}})=0
, which leads to
\bar{\mathcal{E}}(\Upsilon_{\hat{\theta}},\Upsilon_{\pi})=O_{p}(1/\sqrt{N})
. On the other hand, under non-realizability, we have irreducible term
C_{{\rm sup}}B_{1}(\gamma;1,1)\cdot\inf_{\theta\in\Theta}\bar{\mathcal{E}}(\Upsilon_{\theta_{*}},\mathcal{T}^{\pi}\Upsilon_{\theta_{*}})>0
that does not shrink with sample size.

A.9.2
Proof

Even when we replace Assumption
2.1
with Assumption
A.6
, Theorem
2.2
still holds. This can be easily seen, since Assumption
2.1
is used only to show the statement of Assumption
A.6
. It is actually the statement of Assumption
A.6
that we used in proof.

By Theorem
2.2
(or (
21
)), we have
\bar{\mathcal{E}}(\Upsilon_{\hat{\theta}},\Upsilon_{\pi})\leq B(\gamma;\beta_{0},q)\cdot\bar{\mathcal{E}}(\Upsilon_{\hat{\theta}},\mathcal{T}^{\pi}\Upsilon_{\hat{\theta}})
. So, it suffices to show the convergence of
\bar{\mathcal{E}}(\Upsilon_{\hat{\theta}},\mathcal{T}^{\pi}\Upsilon_{\hat{\theta}})\rightarrow^{P}0
. With
\theta_{*}:=\arg\min_{\theta\in\Theta}\bar{\mathcal{E}}(\Upsilon_{\theta},\mathcal{T}^{\pi}\Upsilon_{\theta})
, we have
\Upsilon_{\theta_{*}}=\Upsilon_{\pi}
by Assumption
3.1
. This leads to following,

\displaystyle\bar{\mathcal{E}}(\Upsilon_{\hat{\theta}},\mathcal{T}^{\pi}\Upsilon_{\hat{\theta}})

\displaystyle\leq X_{\hat{\theta}}+|X_{\hat{\theta}}-\bar{\mathcal{E}}(\Upsilon_{\hat{\theta}},\mathcal{T}^{\pi}\Upsilon_{\hat{\theta}})|

\displaystyle\leq X_{\theta_{*}}+|X_{\hat{\theta}}-\bar{\mathcal{E}}(\Upsilon_{\hat{\theta}},\mathcal{T}^{\pi}\Upsilon_{\hat{\theta}})|\quad\text{by the definition of }\hat{\theta}\text{ in \eqref{Xtheta_definition}.}

\displaystyle\leq\bar{\mathcal{E}}(\Upsilon_{\theta_{*}},\mathcal{T}^{\pi}\Upsilon_{\theta_{*}})+|X_{\theta_{*}}-\bar{\mathcal{E}}(\Upsilon_{\theta_{*}},\mathcal{T}^{\pi}\Upsilon_{\theta_{*}})|+|X_{\hat{\theta}}-\bar{\mathcal{E}}(\Upsilon_{\hat{\theta}},\mathcal{T}^{\pi}\Upsilon_{\hat{\theta}})|

\displaystyle\leq\bar{\mathcal{E}}(\Upsilon_{\theta_{*}},\mathcal{T}^{\pi}\Upsilon_{\theta_{*}})+2\cdot\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}|X_{\theta}-\bar{\mathcal{E}}(\Upsilon_{\theta},\mathcal{T}^{\pi}\Upsilon_{\theta})|.

Thus, we have the following by (
21
) with
q=1
and
\beta_{0}=1
(see Section
A.3
),

\displaystyle\bar{\mathcal{E}}(\Upsilon_{\hat{\theta}},\Upsilon_{\pi})\leq C_{{\rm sup}}B_{1}(\gamma;1,1)\cdot\bigg\{\inf_{\theta\in\Theta}\bar{\mathcal{E}}(\Upsilon_{\theta},\mathcal{T}^{\pi}\Upsilon_{\theta},)+2\cdot\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}|X_{\theta}-\bar{\mathcal{E}}(\Upsilon_{\theta},\mathcal{T}^{\pi}\Upsilon_{\theta})|\bigg\}.

(47)

We can rewrite the population objective function as follows. As mentioned below (
5
), Using the notation
b_{\mu}
as a probability measure,

\displaystyle\bar{\mathcal{E}}(\Upsilon_{\theta},\mathcal{T}^{\pi}\Upsilon_{\theta})=\int_{\mathcal{S\times A}}\mathcal{E}\bigg\{\Upsilon_{\theta}(s,a),\mathcal{T}^{\pi}\Upsilon_{\theta}(s,a)\bigg\}\mathrm{d}b_{\mu}(s,a)=2A_{\theta}^{(1)}-A_{\theta}^{(2)}-A_{\theta}^{(3)},

\displaystyle A_{\theta}^{(1)}:=\mathbb{E}\|Z_{\alpha}(S,A;\theta)-R-\gamma Z_{\beta}(S^{\prime},A^{\prime};\theta)\|,\quad A_{\theta}^{(2)}:=\mathbb{E}\|Z_{\alpha}(S,A;\theta)-Z_{\beta}(S,A;\theta)\|,

\displaystyle A_{\theta}^{(3)}:=\mathbb{E}\|\gamma Z_{\alpha}(S^{\prime},A_{\alpha}^{\prime};\theta)-\gamma Z_{\beta}(S^{\prime},A_{\beta}^{\prime};\theta)\|,

where
(S,A,R,S^{\prime})
is iid generated from composition of
S,A\sim b_{\mu}
followed by
R,S^{\prime}\sim p(\cdots|S,A)
(no randomness)
and
A_{\alpha}^{\prime},A_{\beta}^{\prime}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\sim}\limits^{iid}}\pi(\cdot|S^{\prime})
. Then, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}|X_{\theta}-\bar{\mathcal{E}}(\Upsilon_{\theta},\mathcal{T}^{\pi}\Upsilon_{\theta})|\leq 2\cdot\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}|X_{\theta}^{(1)}-A_{\theta}^{(1)}|+\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}|X_{\theta}^{(2)}-A_{\theta}^{(2)}|+\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}|X_{\theta}^{(3)}-A_{\theta}^{(3)}|.

Let us handle the first term.

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}|X_{\theta}^{(1)}-A_{\theta}^{(1)}|\leq\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}|(X_{\theta}^{(1)}-A_{\theta}^{(1)})-(X_{\theta_{0}}^{(1)}-A_{\theta_{0}}^{(1)})|+|X_{\theta_{0}}^{(1)}-A_{\theta_{0}}^{(1)}|.

We can express
X_{\theta}^{(1)}-A_{\theta}^{(1)}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}W_{i}^{\theta}
where the realization of
W_{i}^{\theta}
is written as

\displaystyle w_{i}^{\theta}:=\mathbb{E}\|Z_{\alpha}(s_{i},a_{i};\theta)-r_{i}-\gamma Z_{\beta}(s_{i}^{\prime},A_{i}^{\prime};\theta)\|-\mathbb{E}\|Z_{\alpha}(S,A;\theta)-R-\gamma Z_{\beta}(S^{\prime},A^{\prime};\theta)\|.

Now, we repeat the logic that we used in Section
A.7.3
. We have
\mathbb{E}(W_{i}^{\theta})=0
and
|w_{i}^{\theta_{1}}-w_{i}^{\theta_{2}}|\leq 4\cdot\mathbb{W}_{1,\infty}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})
by the same logic with Appendix
A.6
. Then we apply Dudley’s inequality (Theorem 8.1.6 of
Vershynin 2018
) to obtain the following: with probability larger than
1-2\exp(-u^{2})
,

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}|(X_{\theta}^{(1)}-A_{\theta}^{(1)})

\displaystyle-(X_{\theta_{0}}^{(1)}-A_{\theta_{0}}^{(1)})|\leq\frac{C_{1}}{\sqrt{N}}\cdot\bigg\{\int_{0}^{\infty}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{N}(\Theta,\mathbb{W}_{1,\infty},t)}\mathrm{d}t+u\cdot{\rm diam}(\Theta;\mathbb{W}_{1,\infty})\bigg\}.

We can also apply Theorem 2.6.2 of
Vershynin 2018
to obtain the following by the same logic as (
35
),

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(|X_{\theta_{0}}^{(1)}-A_{\theta_{0}}^{(1)}|\geq\epsilon)

\displaystyle\leq 2\cdot\exp\bigg\{\frac{-C_{1}\cdot N\cdot\epsilon^{2}}{\|W_{1}^{\theta_{0}}-\mathbb{E}(W_{1}^{\theta_{0}})\|_{\psi_{2}}^{2}}\bigg\}\leq 2\cdot\exp(-C_{2}\cdot M\cdot\epsilon^{2}/C_{mod}^{2}).

We can apply the same logic for
\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}|X_{\theta}^{(2)}-A_{\theta}^{(2)}|
and
\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}|X_{\theta}^{(3)}-A_{\theta}^{(3)}|
. Then, we eventually obtain
the following with probability larger than
1-\delta

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}|

\displaystyle X_{\theta}-\bar{\mathcal{E}}(\Upsilon_{\theta},\mathcal{T}^{\pi}\Upsilon_{\theta})|\leq\frac{C_{5}}{\sqrt{N}}\cdot\bigg\{\int_{0}^{\infty}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{N}(\Theta,\mathbb{W}_{1,\infty},t)}\mathrm{d}t+C_{mod}\cdot\{{\rm diam}(\Theta;\mathbb{W}_{1,\infty})+1\}\cdot\sqrt{\log\bigg(\frac{12}{\delta}\bigg)}\bigg\}.

Combined with (
47
), we have the desired result.

A.10
Comparison With FLE

As we mentioned in Remark
3.5
, we will assume
d=1
, tabular setting (
|\mathcal{S\times A}|<\infty
), and bounded rewards.

\displaystyle\text{EBRM : }\mathbb{W}_{p}(\Upsilon_{\hat{\theta}}^{marginal},\Upsilon_{\pi}^{marginal})\leq O_{p}(N^{\frac{-1/p}{2+\alpha}})\quad\text{VS}\quad\text{FLE : }\mathbb{W}_{p}(\Upsilon_{\hat{\theta}}^{marginal},\Upsilon_{\pi})\leq O_{p}(N^{\frac{-1}{2p}(1-\alpha)}).

(48)

Following is the proof. We first define the following two terms,

\displaystyle F(\theta)=\bar{\mathcal{E}}(\Upsilon_{\theta},\mathcal{T}^{\pi},\Upsilon_{\theta})\quad\&\quad\hat{F}(\theta)=\hat{\bar{\mathcal{E}}}(\Upsilon_{\theta},\hat{\mathcal{T}}^{\pi}\Upsilon_{\theta}).

Then we have following. The data
\{s_{i},a_{i},r_{i},s_{i}^{\prime}\}_{i=1}^{N}
are the observations that we are given with. Conditioned on a fixed
s,a
,
\{r_{i}(s,a),s_{i}^{\prime}(s,a)\}_{i=1}^{N(s,a)}
are the observations that we observed for that particular
s,a
.

\displaystyle\hat{F}(\theta)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\bigg\{\underbrace{2\cdot\mathbb{E}\|Z_{\alpha}(s_{i},a_{i};\theta)-r_{i}-\gamma Z_{\beta}(s_{i}^{\prime},A_{i}^{\prime};\theta)\|-\mathbb{E}\|Z_{\alpha}(s_{i},a_{i};\theta)-Z_{\beta}(s_{i},a_{i};\theta)\|}_{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt=}\limits^{let}}A_{i}^{\theta}}\bigg\}

\displaystyle-\sum_{s,a}\frac{N(s,a)}{N}\cdot\frac{1}{N(s,a)^{2}}\sum_{i,j=1}^{N(s,a)}\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\|r_{i}(s,a)+\gamma Z_{\alpha}(s_{i}^{\prime}(s,a),A_{i}^{\prime}(s,a);\theta)-r_{j}(s,a)-\gamma Z_{\beta}(s_{j}^{\prime}(s,a),A_{j}^{\prime}(s,a);\theta)\|}_{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt=}\limits^{let}}C_{ij}^{\theta}(s,a)}.

Then we have

\displaystyle\Delta_{N}(\theta)

\displaystyle:=\big(\hat{F}(\theta)-F(\theta)\big)-\big(\hat{F}({\theta_{*}})-F({\theta_{*}})\big)

\displaystyle=\bigg\{\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}(A_{i}^{\theta}-A_{i}^{\theta})-\mathbb{E}(A_{1}^{\theta}-A_{1}^{\theta_{*}})\bigg\}

\displaystyle\quad-\bigg\{\sum_{s,a}\frac{N(s,a)}{N}\cdot\frac{1}{N(s,a)^{2}}\cdot\sum_{i,j=1}^{N(s,a)}\big(C_{ij}^{\theta}(s,a)-C_{ij}^{\theta_{*}}(s,a)\big)-\sum_{s,a}b_{\mu}(s,a)\cdot\mathbb{E}\big(C_{12}^{\theta}(s,a)-C_{12}^{\theta_{*}}(s,a)\big)\bigg\}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\bigg\{\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}(A_{i}^{\theta}-A_{i}^{\theta_{*}})-\mathbb{E}(A_{i}^{\theta}-A_{i}^{\theta_{*}})\bigg\}}_{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt=}\limits^{let}}\Delta_{N}^{(1)}}

\displaystyle\quad-\underbrace{\bigg\{\sum_{s,a}\bigg(\frac{N(s,a)}{N}-b_{\mu}(s,a)\bigg)\cdot\frac{1}{N(s,a)^{2}}\cdot\sum_{i,j=1}^{N(s,a)}\big(C_{ij}^{\theta}(s,a)-C_{ij}^{\theta_{*}}(s,a)\big)\bigg\}}_{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt=}\limits^{let}}\Delta_{N}^{(2)}(\theta)}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{s,a}b_{\mu}(s,a)\cdot\underbrace{\bigg\{\frac{1}{N(s,a)^{2}}\sum_{i,j=1}^{N(s,a)}\big(C_{ij}^{\theta}(s,a)-C_{ij}^{\theta_{*}}(s,a)\big)-\mathbb{E}\big(C_{12}^{\theta}(s,a)-C_{12}^{\theta_{*}}(s,a)\big)\bigg\}}_{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt=}\limits^{let}}\Delta_{N}^{(3)}(\theta;s,a)}

Denote the last term above as
\Delta_{N}^{(3)}(\theta)=\sum_{s,a}b_{\mu}(s,a)\cdot\Delta_{N}^{(3)}(\theta;s,a)
. Let us bound the following three terms respectively.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg\{\sup_{\mathbb{W}_{1,\infty}(\theta,\theta_{*})\leq\delta}|\Delta_{N}(\theta)|\bigg\}\leq\mathbb{E}\bigg\{\sup_{\mathbb{W}_{1,\infty}(\theta,\theta_{*})\leq\delta}|\Delta_{N}^{(1)}(\theta)|\bigg\}+\mathbb{E}\bigg\{\sup_{\mathbb{W}_{1,\infty}(\theta,\theta_{*})\leq\delta}|\Delta_{N}^{(2)}(\theta)|\bigg\}+\mathbb{E}\bigg\{\sup_{\mathbb{W}_{1,\infty}(\theta,\theta_{*})\leq\delta}|\Delta_{N}^{(3)}(\theta)|\bigg\}

Let us start with the first one. With a universal constant
C>0
, we can apply Lemma 2.6.8 of
Vershynin 2018
to obtain the following,

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{N}^{(1)}({\theta_{1}})-\Delta_{N}^{(2)}({\theta_{2}})\|_{\psi_{2}}=\bigg\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}(A_{i}^{\theta_{1}}-A_{i}^{\theta_{2}})-\mathbb{E}(A_{1}^{\theta}-A_{1}^{\theta_{2}})\bigg\|_{\psi_{2}}\leq C\cdot\bigg\|\frac{1}{N}(A_{i}^{\theta_{1}}-A_{i}^{\theta_{2}})\bigg\|_{\psi_{2}}.

Since we have
|A_{i}^{\theta_{1}}-A_{i}^{\theta_{2}}|\leq 4\cdot\mathbb{W}_{1,\infty}({\theta_{1}},{\theta_{2}})
(shown in the proof of previous sections), this leads to following by Proposition 2.6.1, Example 2.5.8, Theorem 8.1.3 of
Vershynin 2018
. With
\Theta^{\delta}:=\{\theta\in\Theta:\mathbb{W}_{1,\infty}(\theta,{\theta_{*}})\leq\delta\}
, we have

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{N}^{(1)}({\theta_{1}})-\Delta_{N}^{(2)}({\theta_{2}})\|_{\psi_{2}}\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{N}}\cdot\mathbb{W}_{1,\infty}({\theta_{1}},{\theta_{2}}),

\displaystyle\therefore\ \mathbb{E}\bigg\{{\sup_{\theta\in\Theta^{\delta}}}|\Delta_{N}^{(1)}(\theta)|\bigg\}\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{N}}\cdot\int_{0}^{\infty}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{N}(\Theta^{\delta},\mathbb{W}_{1,\infty},\epsilon)}\mathrm{d}\epsilon\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{N}}\cdot\int_{0}^{\infty}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{N}(\Theta,\mathbb{W}_{1,\infty},\epsilon)}\mathrm{d}\epsilon.

Now, let us assume
\log\mathcal{N}(\Theta,\mathbb{W}_{1,\infty},\epsilon)\leq C_{met}\cdot\epsilon^{-\alpha}
for some
\alpha\in(0,2)
. Then, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg\{{\sup_{\theta\in\Theta^{\delta}}}|\Delta_{N}^{(1)}(\theta)|\bigg\}\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{N}}\cdot\frac{C_{met}^{1/2}}{1-\alpha/2}\cdot\delta^{1-\alpha/2}.

Now let us begin with the second term,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg\{{\sup_{\theta\in\Theta^{\delta}}}|\Delta_{N}^{(2)}(\theta)|\bigg\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigg\{{\sup_{\theta\in\Theta^{\delta}}}\bigg|\sum_{s,a}\bigg(\frac{N(s,a)}{N}-b_{\mu}(s,a)\bigg)\cdot\frac{1}{N(s,a)^{2}}\sum_{i,j=1}^{N(s,a)}\big(C_{ij}^{\theta}(s,a)-C_{ij}^{\theta_{*}}(s,a)\big)\bigg|\bigg\}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\bigg\{{\sup_{\theta\in\Theta^{\delta}}}\sum_{s,a}\bigg|\frac{N(s,a)}{N}-b_{\mu}(s,a)\bigg|\cdot\frac{1}{N(s,a)^{2}}\sum_{i,j=1}^{N(s,a)}\underbrace{\bigg|C_{ij}^{\theta}(s,a)-C_{ij}^{\theta_{*}}(s,a)\bigg|}_{\leq 2\cdot\mathbb{W}_{1,\infty}(\theta,{\theta_{*}})}\bigg\}

\displaystyle\leq 2\delta\cdot\sum_{s,a}\mathbb{E}\bigg|\frac{N(s,a)}{N}-b_{\mu}(s,a)\bigg|\leq C\cdot\delta\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\cdot|\mathcal{S\times A}|.

Now let us bound the third term,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg\{{\sup_{\theta\in\Theta^{\delta}}}|\Delta_{N}^{(3)}(\theta)|\bigg\}=\mathbb{E}\bigg\{{\sup_{\theta\in\Theta^{\delta}}}|\sum_{s,a}b_{\mu}(s,a)\cdot\Delta_{N}^{(3)}(\theta;s,a)|\bigg\}\leq\sum_{s,a}b_{\mu}(s,a)\cdot\mathbb{E}\bigg\{{\sup_{\theta\in\Theta^{\delta}}}|\Delta_{N}^{(3)}(\theta;s,a)|\bigg\}.

For notational convenience, let
n=N(s,a)
and ignore simplify
C_{ij}^{\theta}=C_{ij}^{\theta}(s,a)
.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg\{\Delta_{N}^{(3)}(\theta;s,a)\bigg\}

\displaystyle=\frac{n-1}{n}\cdot\mathbb{E}\bigg\{{\sup_{\theta\in\Theta^{\delta}}}\bigg|\frac{1}{n(n-1)}\sum_{i,j=1}^{N(s,a)}\big((C_{ij}^{\theta}-C_{ij}^{\theta_{*}})-\mathbb{E}(C_{12}^{\theta}-C_{12}^{\theta_{*}})\big)\bigg|\bigg\}

\displaystyle=\frac{n-1}{n}\cdot\mathbb{E}\bigg\{{\sup_{\theta\in\Theta^{\delta}}}\bigg|\underbrace{\frac{1}{n(n-1)}\sum_{i\neq j}\big((C_{ij}^{\theta}-C_{ij}^{\theta_{*}})-\mathbb{E}(C_{12}^{\theta}-C_{12}^{\theta_{*}})\big)}_{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt=}\limits^{let}}\tilde{\Delta}_{n}^{(3)}(\theta;s,a)}\bigg|\bigg\}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{n}\cdot\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\bigg\{{\sup_{\theta\in\Theta^{\delta}}}\bigg|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big((C_{ii}^{\theta}-C_{ii}^{\theta_{*}})-\mathbb{E}(C_{12}^{\theta}-C_{12}^{\theta_{*}})\big)\bigg|\bigg\}}_{\leq 2\delta}.

Now let us bound the term
\mathbb{E}\{{\sup_{\theta\in\Theta^{\delta}}}|\tilde{\Delta}_{n}^{(3)}(\theta;s,a)|\}
as follows with Lemma 2.6.8, Proposition 2.6.1 of
Vershynin 2018
,

\displaystyle\|\tilde{\Delta}_{n}^{(3)}({\theta_{1}};s,a)-\tilde{\Delta}_{n}^{(3)}({\theta_{2}};s,a)\|_{\psi_{2}}=\bigg\|\frac{1}{n(n-1)}\sum_{i\neq j}\big((C_{ij}^{\theta_{1}}-C_{ij}^{\theta_{2}})-\mathbb{E}(C_{12}^{\theta_{1}}-C_{12}^{\theta_{2}})\big)\bigg\|_{\psi_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\cdot\bigg\|\frac{1}{n(n-1)}\sum_{i\neq j}(C_{ij}^{\theta_{1}}-C_{ij}^{\theta_{2}})\bigg\|_{\psi_{2}}\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{n}}\cdot\mathbb{W}_{1,\infty}({\theta_{1}},{\theta_{2}}).

In the last inequality above, we have used Lemma S4 of
Wang
et al. 2022
as we have done in previous proofs. Without loss of generality, we can assume
0<\delta<1
. Conditioned under
\Omega_{\mathcal{S\times A}}^{(\epsilon)}
with
\epsilon=1
, we have
n\geq\frac{1}{2}p_{\rm min}\cdot N
.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg\{{\sup_{\theta\in\Theta^{\delta}}}|\Delta_{N}^{(3)}(\theta;s,a)|\ \bigg|\ \Omega_{\mathcal{S\times A}}^{(\epsilon)}\bigg\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{n}}\cdot\int_{0}^{\infty}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{N}(\Theta,\mathbb{W}_{1,\infty},\epsilon)}\mathrm{d}\epsilon+C\cdot\frac{1}{n}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{p_{\rm min}}}\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\cdot\frac{C_{met}^{1/2}}{1-\alpha/2}\cdot\delta^{1-\alpha/2}+\frac{C}{N\cdot p_{\rm min}},

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\big(\Omega_{\mathcal{S\times A}}^{(\epsilon)}\big)^{c}\bigg\}\leq C_{24}\cdot

\displaystyle\exp(-C_{25}\cdot p_{{\rm min}}^{2}\cdot N)\quad\text{by \eqref{Omega_probability}}.

Combined with
\sup_{s,a}\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}|\Delta_{N}^{(3)}(\theta;s,a)|<\infty
(under bounded rewards assumption), we obtain following,

\displaystyle\therefore\ \mathbb{E}\bigg\{{\sup_{\theta\in\Theta^{\delta}}}|\Delta_{N}^{(3)}(\theta)|\bigg\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{p_{\rm min}}}\cdot\frac{C_{met}^{1/2}}{1-\alpha/2}\cdot\frac{\delta^{1-\alpha/2}}{\sqrt{N}}+\frac{C}{N\cdot p_{\rm min}}\quad\text{for sufficiently large }N.

Putting all three terms together, we finally obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg\{{\sup_{\theta\in\Theta^{\delta}}}|\Delta_{N}(\theta)|\bigg\}\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{N}}\cdot\max\bigg\{\frac{C_{met}^{1/2}}{1-\alpha/2}\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{p_{\rm min}}},|\mathcal{S\times A}|\bigg\}\cdot\delta^{1-\alpha/2}+\frac{C}{p_{\rm min}}\cdot\frac{1}{N}.

Then, we assume
\Upsilon_{\theta}(s,a)
are all 1-dimensional and have bounded support in
[a,b]
for all
s,a\in\mathcal{S\times A}
. By Theorem
2.2
, we obtain the following,

\displaystyle F(\theta)-F({\theta_{*}})=\bar{\mathcal{E}}(\Upsilon_{\theta},\mathcal{T}^{\pi}\Upsilon_{\theta})\geq\frac{1}{B(\gamma)}\cdot\bar{\mathcal{E}}(\Upsilon_{\theta},\Upsilon_{{\theta_{*}}})\geq\frac{1}{B(\gamma)}\cdot\frac{2}{b-a}\cdot\overline{\mathbb{W}_{1}^{2}}\geq\frac{1}{B(\gamma)}\cdot\frac{2}{b-a}\cdot\overline{\mathbb{W}}_{1}^{2}(\theta,{\theta_{*}})

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{B(\gamma)}\cdot\frac{2}{b-a}\cdot p_{\rm min}\cdot\mathbb{W}_{1,\infty}(\theta,{\theta_{*}}).

Here, we have used the following two facts: With
l_{p}
defined in (
23
),

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}(P,Q)\geq 2\cdot l_{2}^{2}(P,Q)\geq\frac{2}{b-a}\cdot l_{1}^{2}(P,Q)=\frac{2}{b-a}\cdot\mathbb{W}_{1}^{2}(P,Q),

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p_{\rm min}}\cdot\overline{\mathbb{W}}_{1}(\theta,{\theta_{*}})=\sum_{s,a}\frac{b_{\mu}(s,a)}{p_{\rm min}}\cdot\mathbb{W}_{1}\big\{\Upsilon_{\theta}(s,a),\Upsilon_{{\theta_{*}}}(s,a)\big\}\geq\mathbb{W}_{1,\infty}(\theta,{\theta_{*}}).

Based on Theorem
C.1
, we obtain the following,

\displaystyle\mathbb{W}_{1,\infty}({\hat{\theta}},{\theta_{*}})\leq C_{A}^{\frac{-1}{1+\alpha/2}}\cdot O_{p}(N^{\frac{1}{2+\alpha}})+C_{B}^{1/2}\cdot O_{p}(N^{-1/2})\leq\max\bigg\{C_{A}^{\frac{1}{1+\alpha/2}},C_{B}^{1/2}\bigg\}\cdot O_{p}(N^{\frac{-1}{2+\alpha}}),\quad\text{where}

\displaystyle C_{A}=C\cdot\max\bigg\{\frac{C_{met}^{1/2}}{1-\alpha/2}\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{p_{\rm min}}},|\mathcal{S\times A}|\bigg\}\quad\&\quad C_{B}=\frac{C}{\sqrt{p_{\rm min}}}.

Using the fact
\mathbb{W}_{p}\leq(b-a)^{1-\frac{1}{p}}\cdot\mathbb{W}_{1}^{1/p}
(from Section 2.3 of
Panaretos and
Zemel 2019
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{W}_{p,\infty}\leq(b-a)^{1-\frac{1}{p}}\cdot\max\bigg\{C_{A}^{\frac{1}{1+\alpha/2}},C_{B}^{1/2}\bigg\}\cdot O_{p}(N^{\frac{-1}{2+\alpha}}).

Now, let us compare the convergence rate between EBRM and FLE
(
Wu
et al. 2023
)
. Let us assume the same model-complexity that they used in Corollary 4.14:

\displaystyle\log\mathcal{N}_{[\ ]}(\tilde{\mathcal{F}}_{\Theta},\|\cdot\|_{\infty,1},\epsilon)\lesssim\epsilon^{-\alpha}\quad\text{for some }\alpha\in(0,2),

where
\tilde{\mathcal{F}}_{\Theta}:=\{f_{\theta}(\cdot|\cdots):s,a-\text{conditioned densities of }\Upsilon_{\theta}\}
and
\|f_{\theta_{1}}-f_{\theta_{2}}\|_{\infty,1}:=\sup_{s,a}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}|f_{\theta_{1}}(z|s,a)-f_{\theta_{2}}(z|s,a)|\mathrm{d}z
. Temporarily ignoring all factors other than
N
, FLE achieves the following rate according to Corollary 4.14 of
Wu
et al. 2023
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{W}_{p}(\Upsilon_{\hat{\theta}}^{marginal},\Upsilon_{\pi})\leq O_{p}(N^{\frac{-1}{2p}})\cdot\big\{\log\mathcal{N}_{[\ ]}(\mathcal{F}_{\Theta},\|\cdot\|_{\infty,1},1/N)\big\}^{\frac{1}{2p}}\leq O_{p}(N^{\frac{-1}{2p}(1-\alpha)}).

For EBRM, we let
l(\cdot|s,a)
and
u(\cdot|s,a)
positive-valued functions (like conditional density functions), and we have

\displaystyle\|L(\cdot|\cdots)-U(\cdot|\cdots)\|_{\infty,1}\leq(b-a)\cdot\|l(\cdot|\cdots)-u(\cdot|\cdots)\|_{\infty,1}

\displaystyle\text{where }L(y|s,a):=\int_{a}^{y}l(z|\cdots)\mathrm{d}z,\quad U(y|\cdots):=\int_{a}^{y}u(z|\cdots)\mathrm{d}z.

Then we obtain the following,

\displaystyle\log\mathcal{N}(\Theta,\mathbb{W}_{1,\infty},\epsilon)\leq\log\mathcal{N}_{[\ ]}(\mathcal{F}_{\Theta},\|\cdot\|_{\infty,1},\epsilon/2)\leq\log\mathcal{N}_{[\ ]}(\tilde{\mathcal{F}}_{\Theta},\|\cdot\|_{\infty,1},\frac{\epsilon}{2(b-a)})\lesssim\epsilon^{-\alpha},

\displaystyle\therefore\ \mathbb{W}_{1}(\Upsilon_{\hat{\theta}}^{marginal},\Upsilon_{\pi}^{marginal})\leq\mathbb{W}_{1,\infty}({\hat{\theta}},{\theta_{*}})=O_{p}(N^{\frac{-1}{2+\alpha}})\quad\&\quad\mathbb{W}_{p}(\Upsilon_{\hat{\theta}}^{marginal},\Upsilon_{\pi}^{marginal})\leq O_{p}(N^{\frac{-1/p}{2+\alpha}}).

For
\forall\alpha\in(0,2)
and
\forall p\geq 1
, EBRM has faster convergence rate than FLE under tabular, 1-dimensional, bounded setting.

Appendix B
PROOFS FOR SECTION
4

As in Appendix
3
, we will use
C_{k}>0
(
k\in\mathbb{N}
) to denote appropriate universal constants throughout the proof.

B.1
Exponential Increasing Rate Of Trajectories

Based on how we defined
\hat{\mathcal{T}}^{\pi}
based on
\hat{p}
(
9
),
(\hat{\mathcal{T}}^{\pi})^{m}\Upsilon_{\theta}(s,a)
utilizes the trajectories of tuples
(s,a,r,s^{\prime})
that can occur consecutively under the estimated probability measure
\hat{p}(\cdots|s,a)
and the target policy
\pi(a|s)
,

\displaystyle\big(s,a,r_{i}^{(1)},s_{i}^{(1)},a_{i}^{(1)},r_{i}^{(2)},s_{i}^{(2)},a_{i}^{(2)},r_{i}^{(3)},s_{i}^{(3)},a_{i}^{(3)},\cdots,r_{i}^{(m)},s_{i}^{(m)}\big),

(49)

\displaystyle\text{where }r_{i}^{(t)},s_{i}^{(t)}\sim\hat{p}(\cdots|s_{i}^{(t-1)},a_{i}^{(t-1)}),\ a_{i}^{(t)}\sim\pi(\cdot|s_{i}^{(t)})\ \text{for }\forall t\geq 1,\ \text{with }(s_{i}^{(0)},a_{i}^{(0)})=(s,a).

Let us first verify how many such trajectories (
49
) can amount to, which start from a common state-action pair
s,a
with length
m=2
.

First there are
N(s,a)
many tuples that can occur in the first step,

\displaystyle(s,a,r_{i}^{(1)},s_{i}^{(1)})\ \ \ \big(1\leq i\leq N(s,a)\big).

Now fix one observation with index
i
, and then there can be
|\mathcal{A}|
many actions at most that can follow
s_{i}^{(1)}
, giving us the following tuples,

\displaystyle(s,a,r_{i}^{(1)},s_{i}^{(1)},a_{1}),\ (s,a,r_{i}^{(1)},s_{i}^{(1)},a_{2}),\ \cdots,\ (s,a,r_{i}^{(1)},s_{i}^{(1)},a_{|\mathcal{A}|})\ \ \ \big(1\leq i\leq N(s,a)\big).

Now we are given with
|\mathcal{A}|
different state-action pairs,
(s_{i}^{(1)},a_{1}),\cdots,(s_{i}^{(1)},a_{|\mathcal{A}|})
, and then the following observations of
(r_{i}^{(2)},s_{i}^{(2)})
can be as many as
\sum_{k=1}^{|\mathcal{A}|}N(s_{i}^{(1)},a_{k})\leq\sum_{s,a}N(s,a)=N
.
This eventually gives us at most
N(s,a)\times N
trajectories of length
m=2
starting from the given state-action pair
s,a
,

\displaystyle(s,a,r_{i}^{(1)},s_{i}^{(1)},a_{i}^{(1)},r_{i}^{(2)},s_{i}^{(2)})\ \ \ \big(1\leq i\leq N(s,a)\times N\big).

Then we can add up for all state-action pairs that we can begin with, which leads to
\sum_{s,a}N(s,a)\times N=N^{2}
many trajectories at most.
We can generalize this result for an arbitrary value of
m\in\mathbb{N}
, which gives us
N(s,a)\times N^{m-1}
many trajectories for a given state-action pair
s,a
, leading to

N^{m}
many trajectories (
49
) of length
m
if we sum them all up for all state-action pairs as the initial point.

B.2
How Supremum-extended Wasserstein-1 Metric Can Be Replaced

One may wonder why supremum-extended (
3
) Wasserstein-1 metric
\mathbb{W}_{1,\infty}
appears in Assumption
4.1
. In fact,
\mathbb{W}_{1,\infty}
can be replaced with a general statistical distance measure, say
\tilde{\eta}:\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\times\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
, if it satisfies the following properties.

1.

\tilde{\eta}
is a metric. It is a contraction with respect to
\tilde{\eta}
, i.e.
\tilde{\eta}(\mathcal{T}^{\pi}\Upsilon_{1},\mathcal{T}^{\pi}\Upsilon_{2})\leq\gamma^{\beta_{0}}\cdot\tilde{\eta}(\Upsilon_{1},\Upsilon_{2})
for some
\beta_{0}>0
, for any
\mathcal{T}^{\pi}
where the corresponding transition probability
p
(
1
) can be arbitrary.

2.

For arbitrary
c\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}\in[0,1]
,
(s,a),(\tilde{s},\tilde{a})\in\mathcal{S\times A}
, letting
Z_{i}(s,a)\sim\Upsilon_{i}(s,a)
be such that
(Z_{1}(s,a),Z_{3}(s,a))\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
(Z_{2}(\tilde{s},\tilde{a}),Z_{4}(\tilde{s},\tilde{a}))\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
are mutually independent,
\tilde{\eta}
should satisfy

\displaystyle\bigg|\mathbb{E}\|c+\gamma_{1}Z_{1}(s,a)-\gamma_{2}Z_{2}(\tilde{s},\tilde{a})\|-\mathbb{E}\|c+

\displaystyle\gamma_{1}Z_{3}(s,a)-\gamma_{2}Z_{4}(\tilde{s},\tilde{a})\|\bigg|\leq\gamma_{1}\cdot\tilde{\eta}(\Upsilon_{1},\Upsilon_{3})+\gamma_{2}\cdot\tilde{\eta}(\Upsilon_{2},\Upsilon_{4}).

Note that
\mathbb{W}_{1,\infty}
satisfies all three properties. It is shown to be a metric in by
Bellemare
et al. 2017
in their Lemma 2. In Appendix
A.6
, we have shown that
\mathbb{W}_{1,\infty}
satisfies the second property. Lastly,
Bellemare
et al. 2017
showed that it makes
\mathcal{T}^{\pi}
a contraction in their Lemma 3. If we could find another distance
\tilde{\eta}
that satisfies all three properties, then we can replace
\mathbb{W}_{1,\infty}
with
\tilde{\eta}
in all proofs in the following subsections (Appendices
B.4
,
B.5
,
B.6
). Of course, if we are to proceed with
\tilde{\eta}
, we will need to replace Assumption
4.1
with
\tilde{\eta}(\Upsilon_{\theta_{1}},\Upsilon_{\theta_{2}})\leq L\|\theta_{1}-\theta_{2}\|
.

B.3
Bootstrap-based Estimation

Let us first discuss how
(\hat{\mathcal{T}}^{\pi})^{m}
is implemented. Generalizing from the definition of
\hat{\mathcal{T}}^{\pi}
based on (
10
), we consider
\hat{Z}^{(m)}(s,a;\theta)\sim(\hat{\mathcal{T}}^{\pi})^{m}\Upsilon_{\theta}(s,a)
as the distribution of an
m
-length trajectories of tuples
(s,a,r,s^{\prime})
that is generated under the estimated transition
\hat{p}
and the target policy
\pi
:

\displaystyle\hat{Z}^{(m)}(s,a;\theta)\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt=}\limits^{D}}\sum_{t=1}^{m}\gamma^{t-1}\hat{R}^{(t)}+\gamma^{m}Z(\hat{S}^{(m)},\hat{A}^{(m)};\theta),

(50)

where
(\hat{R}^{(t)},\hat{S}^{(t)})\sim\hat{p}(\cdots|\hat{S}^{(t-1)},\hat{A}^{(t-1)})
,
\hat{A}^{(t)}\sim\pi(\cdot|\hat{S}^{(t)})
for all
t\geq 1
,
(\hat{S}^{(0)},\hat{A}^{(0)})=(s,a)
.

However, the estimation of the
(\hat{\mathcal{T}}^{\pi})^{m}\Upsilon_{\theta}
(
50
) generally requires the computation of
N^{m}
trajectories (as discussed in Appendix
B.1
), which amounts to a heavy computational burden.

To alleviate such burden, we will instead bootstrap
M\ll N^{m}
many trajectories by first sampling the initial state-action pairs
(s_{i}^{(0)},a_{i}^{(0)})\ (1\leq i\leq M)
from
\hat{b}_{\mu}
and then resampling the subsequent
r_{i}^{(t+1)},s_{i}^{(t+1)}\sim\hat{p}(\cdots|s_{i}^{(t)},a_{i}^{(t)})
and
a_{i}^{(t+1)}\sim\pi(\cdot|s_{i}^{(t+1)})
for
m
steps. Let
\hat{p}_{m}^{(B)}(\cdots|s,a)
be the empirical probability measure of
(\sum_{t=1}^{m}\gamma^{t-1}r_{i}^{(t)},s_{i}^{(m)})
conditioning on
(s_{i}^{(0)},a_{i}^{(0)})=(s,a)
. We define the
bootstrap operator
as follows, with an abuse of notation
\mathcal{B}_{m}Z(s,a;\theta)\sim\mathcal{B}_{m}\Upsilon_{\theta}(s,a)
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{m}Z(s,a;\theta):=\sum_{t=1}^{m}\gamma^{t-1}\hat{R}_{b}^{(t)}+\gamma^{m}Z(\hat{S}_{b}^{(m)},\hat{A}_{b}^{(m)};\theta),

(51)

where we have
(\sum_{t=1}^{m}\gamma^{t-1}\hat{R}_{b}^{(t)},\hat{S}_{b}^{(m)})\sim\hat{p}_{m}^{(B)}(\cdots|s,a)
and
\hat{A}_{b}^{(m)}\sim\pi(\cdot|\hat{S}_{b}^{(m)})
. Then we can compute our objective function and derive the bootstrap-based multi-step estimator:

\displaystyle\hat{F}_{m}^{(B)}(\theta):=\hat{\bar{\mathcal{E}}}\big(\Upsilon_{\theta},\mathcal{B}_{m}\Upsilon_{\theta}\big),\ \hat{\theta}_{m}^{(B)}:=\arg\min_{\theta\in\Theta}\hat{F}_{m}^{(B)}(\theta).

Our final algorithm can be summarized as follows, in Algorithm
2
.

Algorithm 2

EBRM-multi-step

Input:

\Theta
,
\mathcal{D}=\{(s_{i},a_{i},r_{i},s_{i}^{\prime})\}_{i=1}^{N}
,
m
,
M

Output:

\hat{\theta}_{m}^{(B)}

Estimate
\hat{b}_{\mu}
and
\hat{p}
.
\triangleright
Refer to Equation (
9
).

Randomly generate
M
tuples of
(\sum_{t=1}^{m}\gamma^{t-1}r_{i}^{(t)},s_{i}^{(m)})\ (1\leq i\leq M)
.
\triangleright
Refer to Equation (
51
).

\hat{\theta}_{m}^{(B)}=\arg\min_{\theta\in\Theta}\hat{\bar{\mathcal{E}}}(\Upsilon_{\theta},\mathcal{B}_{m}\Upsilon_{\theta})
.

B.4
Estimation Error Of Multi-step Bellman Residual

Lemma B.1
.

(Convergence of estimated Bellman residual)
Under Assumptions
2.1
,
3.2
, and
4.1
, for arbitrary
\epsilon\in(0,1]
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\bigg|\hat{F}_{m}(\theta)-F_{m}(\theta)\bigg|\leq C_{1}\cdot m^{2}\cdot\frac{1}{p_{{\rm min}}^{2}}\cdot C_{\rm env}^{(m)}(\Theta)\cdot\bigg(\frac{1}{N^{1/4}}+\sqrt{\epsilon}\bigg)

with probability larger than

\displaystyle 1-C_{2}\cdot m\cdot(|\mathcal{S\times A}|+N)\cdot\exp\big\{-C_{3}\cdot p_{{\rm min}}^{2}\cdot N\cdot\epsilon^{2}/C_{\rm den}(m)\big\}

with
p_{{\rm min}}>0
defined in Assumption
2.1
,
\theta_{0}\in\Theta
in (
44
),
C_{\rm den}(m)
and
C_{\rm env}^{(m)}(\Theta)
in (
59
).

Although larger values of step level
m
both increase and decrease some terms, the decreasing parts have a non-zero lower bounds
\gamma^{m}\sqrt{p}+1
and
1+\gamma^{m}
of (
59
). Thus it can be seen that increased values of step level
m
eventually leads to looser bound, necessitating larger sample size
N
.

B.4.1
Decomposition

We start with the following decomposition based on definition of
\hat{F}_{m}
in (
18
),

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\bigg|\hat{F}_{m}(\theta)-F_{m}(\theta)\bigg|=\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\bigg|\bar{\mathcal{E}}\big\{\Upsilon_{\theta},(\mathcal{T}^{\pi})^{m}\Upsilon_{\theta}\big\}-\hat{\bar{\mathcal{E}}}\big\{\Upsilon_{\theta},(\hat{\mathcal{T}}^{\pi})^{m}\Upsilon_{\theta}\big\}\bigg|\leq

\displaystyle\underbrace{\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\bigg|\bar{\mathcal{E}}\big\{\Upsilon_{\theta},(\mathcal{T}^{\pi})^{m}\Upsilon_{\theta}\big\}-\bar{\mathcal{E}}\big\{\Upsilon_{\theta},(\hat{\mathcal{T}}^{\pi})^{m}\Upsilon_{\theta}\big\}\bigg|}_{(Term\ 1)}+\underbrace{\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\bigg|\bar{\mathcal{E}}\big\{\Upsilon_{\theta},(\hat{\mathcal{T}}^{\pi})^{m}\Upsilon_{\theta}\big\}-\hat{\bar{\mathcal{E}}}\big\{\Upsilon_{\theta},(\hat{\mathcal{T}}^{\pi})^{m}\Upsilon_{\theta}\big\}\bigg|}_{(Term\ 2)}.

(52)

Unfortunately, we cannot bound
(Term\ 1)
with
\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\bar{\mathcal{E}}((\mathcal{T}^{\pi})^{m}\Upsilon_{\theta},(\hat{\mathcal{T}}^{\pi})^{m}\Upsilon_{\theta})
by applying triangular inequality, since
\bar{\mathcal{E}}
is not a metric. Instead, we can devise an alternative (Lemma
B.2
), based on the fact that
\mathcal{E}
is in fact a squared metric (Property
3
), yet we need to pay price by having square-root. Refer to
C.1.7
for its proof.

Lemma B.2
.

For arbitrary
\Upsilon_{0},\Upsilon_{1},\Upsilon_{2}\in\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{\mathcal{S\times A}}
, we have

\displaystyle\big|\bar{\mathcal{E}}(\Upsilon_{0},\Upsilon_{1})-\bar{\mathcal{E}}(\Upsilon_{0},\Upsilon_{2})\big|\leq 4\cdot\bar{\mathcal{E}}(\Upsilon_{1},\Upsilon_{2})^{1/2}\times\bigg[\max\bigg\{\bar{\mathcal{E}}(\Upsilon_{0},\Upsilon_{1}),\bar{\mathcal{E}}(\Upsilon_{0},\Upsilon_{2})\bigg\}+\bar{\mathcal{E}}(\Upsilon_{1},\Upsilon_{2})\bigg]^{1/2}.

By applying Lemma
B.2
, we obtain the following,

\displaystyle\Gamma_{N,m}:=\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\bar{\mathcal{E}}\big\{(\mathcal{T}^{\pi})^{m}\Upsilon_{\theta},(\hat{\mathcal{T}}^{\pi})^{m}\Upsilon_{\theta}\big\},

\displaystyle(Term\ 1)\leq 12\cdot\Gamma_{N,m}^{1/2}\cdot\bigg\{\Gamma_{N,m}^{1/2}+\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\bar{\mathcal{E}}\big\{\Upsilon_{\theta},(\mathcal{T}^{\pi})^{m}\Upsilon_{\theta}\big\}^{1/2}\bigg\}.

Now let us deal with
(Term\ 2)
that can be decomposed as follows,

\displaystyle(Term\ 2)\leq\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\bigg|\sum_{s,a}\big\{b_{\mu}(s,a)-\hat{b}_{\mu}(s,a)\big\}\cdot\bigg[\mathcal{E}\bigg\{\Upsilon_{\theta}(s,a),(\hat{\mathcal{T}}^{\pi})^{m}\Upsilon_{\theta}(s,a)\bigg\}-\mathcal{E}\bigg\{\Upsilon_{\theta_{0}}(s,a),(\hat{\mathcal{T}}^{\pi})^{m}\Upsilon_{\theta_{0}}(s,a)\bigg\}\bigg]\bigg|

\displaystyle+\bigg|\sum_{s,a}\big\{b_{\mu}(s,a)-\hat{b}_{\mu}(s,a)\big\}\cdot\mathcal{E}\bigg\{\Upsilon_{\theta_{0}}(s,a),(\hat{\mathcal{T}}^{\pi})^{m}\Upsilon_{\theta_{0}}(s,a)\bigg\}\bigg|.

To handle the first line of above decomposition, we can first obtain the following bound by
A.6
and contraction of
\hat{\mathcal{T}}^{\pi}
w.r.t.
\mathbb{W}_{1,\infty}
, where we will use an abuse of notation
\hat{\mathcal{T}}^{\pi}\theta:=\hat{\mathcal{T}}^{\pi}\Upsilon_{\theta}
,

\displaystyle\bigg|\mathcal{E}\bigg\{\Upsilon_{\theta}(s,a),(\hat{\mathcal{T}}^{\pi})^{m}\Upsilon_{\theta}(s,a)\bigg\}-\mathcal{E}\bigg\{\Upsilon_{\theta_{0}}(s,a),(\hat{\mathcal{T}}^{\pi})^{m}\Upsilon_{\theta_{0}}(s,a)\bigg\}\bigg|\leq 4\cdot\mathbb{W}_{1,\infty}(\theta,\theta_{0})+4\cdot\mathbb{W}_{1,\infty}\big\{(\hat{\mathcal{T}}^{\pi})^{m}\theta,(\hat{\mathcal{T}}^{\pi})^{m}\theta_{0}\big\}

\displaystyle\leq 4(1+\gamma^{m})\cdot\mathbb{W}_{1,\infty}(\theta,\theta_{0})\leq 4(1+\gamma^{m})\cdot{\rm diam}(\Theta;\mathbb{W}_{1,\infty})

(53)

where the second last line holds, since a Bellman operator is a contraction with respect to
\mathbb{W}_{1,\infty}
, as we mentioned under (
15
) (proof by
Bellemare
et al. 2017
in their Lemma 3). Further applying (
24
) on the second line of
(Term2)
decomposition,
we can take up Decomposition (
52
) as follows,

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\bigg|\hat{F}_{m}(\theta)-F_{m}(\theta)\bigg|\leq 12\cdot\Gamma_{N,m}^{1/2}\cdot\bigg\{\Gamma_{N,m}^{1/2}+\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\bar{\mathcal{E}}\big\{\Upsilon_{\theta},(\mathcal{T}^{\pi})^{m}\Upsilon_{\theta}\big\}^{1/2}\bigg\}

\displaystyle+4\cdot\sum_{s,a}\bigg|b_{\mu}(s,a)-\hat{b}_{\mu}(s,a)\bigg|\cdot\bigg[(1+\gamma^{m})\cdot{\rm diam}(\Theta;\mathbb{W}_{1,\infty})

\displaystyle+\sup_{s,a}\mathcal{E}\bigg\{\Upsilon_{\theta_{0}}(s,a),(\mathcal{T}^{\pi})^{m}\Upsilon_{\theta_{0}}(s,a)\bigg\}+\sup_{s,a}\mathcal{E}\bigg\{(\mathcal{T}^{\pi})^{m}\Upsilon_{\theta_{0}}(s,a),(\hat{\mathcal{T}}^{\pi})^{m}\Upsilon_{\theta_{0}}(s,a)\bigg\}\bigg].

Here, we can further simplify two terms,
\sup_{s,a}\mathcal{E}\big\{(\mathcal{T}^{\pi})^{m}\Upsilon_{\theta_{0}}(s,a),(\hat{\mathcal{T}}^{\pi})^{m}\Upsilon_{\theta_{0}}(s,a)\big\}
and
\Gamma_{N,m}
. First let
\big(\hat{S}^{(t)}(s,a),\hat{A}^{(t)}(s,a)\big)
be the
t
-th state-action pair that follows the distribution (
50
), that is the random state-action pair which can be reached by consecutively simulating from the estimated probability
\hat{p}(\cdots|s,a)
and the target policy
\pi(a|s)
starting from the initial state-action pair
s,a
. Furthermore, let us denote such probability (density) as
\hat{q}_{b_{\mu}}^{\pi:t}(\cdots|s,a)
that is conditioned on a fixed initial state-action pair
s,a
, and denote the marginalized probability as
\hat{q}_{b_{\mu}}^{\pi:t}(\cdots)
that treats the initial state-action pair
S,A\sim b_{\mu}(s,a)
as random. This aligns with the notation
q_{b_{\mu}}^{\pi:t}(\cdots)
defined below Assumption
2.1
. Then we have the following bound,

\displaystyle\sup_{s,a}\mathcal{E}\bigg\{(\mathcal{T}^{\pi})^{m}\Upsilon_{\theta_{0}}(s,a),(\hat{\mathcal{T}}^{\pi})^{m}\Upsilon_{\theta_{0}}(s,a)\bigg\}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{s,a}\bigg[m\cdot\sum_{t=0}^{m-1}\mathcal{E}\bigg\{(\hat{\mathcal{T}}^{\pi})^{m-t}(\mathcal{T}^{\pi})^{t}\Upsilon_{\theta_{0}}(s,a),(\hat{\mathcal{T}}^{\pi})^{m-t-1}(\mathcal{T}^{\pi})^{t+1}\Upsilon_{\theta_{0}}(s,a)\bigg\}\bigg]\ \ \text{by \eqref{relaxed_triangle_p}}

\displaystyle\leq m\cdot\sum_{t=0}^{m-1}\gamma^{m-t-1}\cdot\sup_{s,a}\mathcal{E}\bigg\{\hat{\mathcal{T}}^{\pi}(\mathcal{T}^{\pi})^{t}\Upsilon_{\theta_{0}}(s,a),\mathcal{T}^{\pi}(\mathcal{T}^{\pi})^{t}\Upsilon_{\theta_{0}}(s,a)\bigg\}.

We also have following by the same logic of Appendix
A.2
, where the subscripts of
\tilde{\mathbb{E}}_{\hat{q}_{b_{\mu}}^{\pi:{m-t-1}}}
and
\tilde{\mathbb{E}}_{b_{\mu}}
indicate the distribution of
S,A
(based on definition of
\tilde{\mathbb{E}}
(
11
) that considers
\hat{\mathcal{T}}^{\pi}
as nonrandom),

\displaystyle\Gamma_{N,m}\leq\frac{m}{p_{{\rm min}}}\cdot\sum_{t=0}^{m-1}\gamma^{m-t-1}\cdot\bigg[\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\bigg|\bar{\mathcal{E}}\big\{\hat{\mathcal{T}}^{\pi}(\mathcal{T}^{\pi})^{t}\Upsilon_{\theta},\mathcal{T}^{\pi}(\mathcal{T}^{\pi})^{t}\Upsilon_{\theta}\big\}-\bar{\mathcal{E}}\big\{\hat{\mathcal{T}}^{\pi}(\mathcal{T}^{\pi})^{t}\Upsilon_{\theta_{0}},\mathcal{T}^{\pi}(\mathcal{T}^{\pi})^{t}\Upsilon_{\theta_{0}}\big\}\bigg|

\displaystyle+\sup_{s,a}\mathcal{E}\bigg\{\hat{\mathcal{T}}^{\pi}(\mathcal{T}^{\pi})^{t}\Upsilon_{\theta_{0}}(s,a),\mathcal{T}^{\pi}(\mathcal{T}^{\pi})^{t}\Upsilon_{\theta_{0}}(s,a)\bigg\}\bigg],

(54)

which is based on
\sup_{s,a}\{\hat{q}_{b_{\mu}}^{\pi:{m-t-1}}(s,a)/b_{\mu}(s,a)\}\leq 1/p_{{\rm min}}
.
Then we can bound
\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}|\hat{F}_{m}(\theta)-F_{m}(\theta)|
by

\displaystyle 12\cdot\bigg\{\frac{m}{p_{{\rm min}}}\cdot\sum_{t=0}^{m-1}\gamma^{m-t-1}\cdot\big(Y_{t}^{(1)}+Y_{t}^{(2)}\big)\bigg\}^{1/2}\times\bigg[\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{\theta}^{1/2}+\bigg\{\frac{m}{p_{{\rm min}}}\cdot\sum_{t=0}^{m-1}\gamma^{m-t-1}\cdot(Y_{t}^{(1)}+Y_{t}^{(2)})\bigg\}^{1/2}\bigg]

(55)

\displaystyle+4\cdot\sum_{s,a}\bigg|b_{\mu}(s,a)-\hat{b}_{\mu}(s,a)\bigg|\times\bigg\{(1+\gamma^{m})\cdot{\rm diam}(\Theta;\mathbb{W}_{1,\infty})+\sup_{s,a}\mathcal{E}_{\theta_{0}}(s,a)+m\cdot\sum_{t=0}^{m-1}\gamma^{m-t-1}\cdot Y_{t}^{(1)}\bigg\}

where each term is defined as

\displaystyle Y_{t}^{(1)}:=\sup_{s,a}\mathcal{E}\bigg\{\hat{\mathcal{T}}^{\pi}(\mathcal{T}^{\pi})^{t}\Upsilon_{\theta_{0}}(s,a),\mathcal{T}^{\pi}(\mathcal{T}^{\pi})^{t}\Upsilon_{\theta_{0}}(s,a)\bigg\},

\displaystyle Y_{t}^{(2)}:=\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\bigg|\bar{\mathcal{E}}\big\{\hat{\mathcal{T}}^{\pi}(\mathcal{T}^{\pi})^{t}\Upsilon_{\theta},\mathcal{T}^{\pi}(\mathcal{T}^{\pi})^{t}\Upsilon_{\theta}\big\}-\bar{\mathcal{E}}\big\{\hat{\mathcal{T}}^{\pi}(\mathcal{T}^{\pi})^{t}\Upsilon_{\theta_{0}},\mathcal{T}^{\pi}(\mathcal{T}^{\pi})^{t}\Upsilon_{\theta_{0}}\big\}\bigg|,

\displaystyle\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{\theta}:=\bar{\mathcal{E}}\big\{\Upsilon_{\theta},(\mathcal{T}^{\pi})^{m}\Upsilon_{\theta}\big\}\ \ \ \&\ \ \ \mathcal{E}_{\theta_{0}}(s,a):=\mathcal{E}\bigg\{\Upsilon_{\theta_{0}}(s,a),(\mathcal{T}^{\pi})^{m}\Upsilon_{\theta_{0}}(s,a)\bigg\}.

B.4.2
Derivation Of Bound

Now we can see that there exist three random quantities:
\sum_{s,a}|b_{\mu}(s,a)-\hat{b}_{\mu}(s,a)|
,
Y_{t}^{(1)}
,
Y_{t}^{(2)}
for each
t\in\{0,1,\cdots,m-1\}
.
We can employ similar techniques that we used in Appendix
A.7
. We will again assume
\Omega_{\mathcal{S\times A}}^{(\epsilon)}
of Definition (
29
), and utilize the conditional probability
\mathbb{P}^{(\mathbf{N})}(\cdots)
. Under
\Omega_{\mathcal{S\times A}}^{(\epsilon)}\ (\epsilon\in(0,1])
, whose probability is larger than

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{\mathcal{S\times A}}^{(\epsilon)})\geq 1-C_{1}\cdot\exp(-C_{2}\cdot p_{{\rm min}}^{2}\cdot N\cdot\epsilon^{2}),

we can verify by Appendix
A.7.4
and (
41
),

\displaystyle\sum_{s,a}\bigg|b_{\mu}(s,a)-\hat{b}_{\mu}(s,a)\bigg|\leq\frac{\sqrt{p_{{\rm min}}}}{2}\cdot\epsilon\ \ \ \text{for }\epsilon\in(0,1].

The remaining two terms
Y_{t}^{(1)}
and
Y_{t}^{(2)}
are merely repetitions of what we showed in
A.7.3
that required Assumption
3.2
and
4.1
, since they are in fact

\displaystyle Y_{t}^{(1)}=\sup_{s,a}X_{\theta_{0}}^{(t)}(s,a),\ \text{where }X_{\theta}^{(t)}(s,a):=\mathcal{E}\bigg\{\hat{\mathcal{T}}^{\pi}\Upsilon_{\theta}^{(t)}(s,a),\mathcal{T}^{\pi}\Upsilon_{\theta}^{(t)}(s,a)\bigg\},\ \ \Upsilon_{\theta}^{(t)}:=(\mathcal{T}^{\pi})^{t}\Upsilon_{\theta},

\displaystyle Y_{t}^{(2)}=\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\bigg|X_{\theta}^{(t)}-X_{\theta_{0}}^{(t)}\bigg|,\ \ \ \text{where }\ X_{\theta}^{(t)}:=\bar{\mathcal{E}}\big(\hat{\mathcal{T}}^{\pi}\Upsilon_{\theta}^{(t)},\mathcal{T}^{\pi}\Upsilon_{\theta}^{(t)}\big)=\sum_{s,a}b_{\mu}(s,a)\cdot X_{\theta}^{(t)}(s,a),

where the notations align with Definition (
31
) of
X_{\theta}
and
X_{\theta}(s,a)
. The proofs are exactly the same except that
Z
is replaced with
Z^{(t)}
in definitions of
W_{i}^{\theta}
,
W_{ij}^{\theta}
, where
Z^{(t)}(s,a;\theta)\sim(\mathcal{T}^{\pi})^{t}\Upsilon_{\theta}(s,a)
.
We can utilize the probability bounds (
34
) and (
36
). Let us first allow the following abuse of notation
(\mathcal{T}^{\pi})^{t}\Theta
, which we will define as

\displaystyle(\mathcal{T}^{\pi})^{t}\Theta:=\bigg\{(\mathcal{T}^{\pi})^{t}\theta\ :\ \theta\in\Theta\bigg\}\ \ \text{for }\forall t\in\{0,1,2,\cdots,m-1\},\ \ \text{where }\mathcal{T}^{\pi}\theta:=\mathcal{T}^{\pi}\Upsilon_{\theta}.

(56)

In order to further bound the metric entropy and the diameter based on (
56
), we can develop a new metric,

\displaystyle\mathbb{W}_{1,\infty}^{(t)}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2}):=\mathbb{W}_{1,\infty}\big\{(\mathcal{T}^{\pi})^{t}\theta_{1},(\mathcal{T}^{\pi})^{t}\theta_{2}\big\}\leq\gamma^{t}\cdot\mathbb{W}_{1,\infty}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2}),

\displaystyle\therefore\ \mathcal{N}\big((\mathcal{T}^{\pi})^{t}\Theta,\mathbb{W}_{1,\infty},z\big)=\mathcal{N}\big(\Theta,\mathbb{W}_{1,\infty}^{(t)},z\big).

Since it satisfies
\gamma^{t}
-Lipschitz continuity w.r.t.
\mathbb{W}_{1,\infty}
, we can apply the logic in Appendix
C.1.6
to obtain

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{N}\big((\mathcal{T}^{\pi})^{t}\Theta,\mathbb{W}_{1,\infty},z\big)}\mathrm{d}z\leq\gamma^{t}\cdot\int_{0}^{\infty}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{N}(\Theta,\mathbb{W}_{1,\infty},t)}\mathrm{d}t,

\displaystyle{\rm diam}\big((\mathcal{T}^{\pi})^{t}\Theta;\mathbb{W}_{1,\infty}\big)\leq\gamma^{t}\cdot{\rm diam}(\Theta;\mathbb{W}_{1,\infty}).

Applying the same logic that we used in Appendix
A.7
, conditioned under
\Omega_{\mathcal{S\times A}}^{(\epsilon)}
(
29
), we can derive the following for arbitrary
\epsilon\in(0,1]
,
\epsilon_{1}>0
,
u>0
, for each
t\in\{0,\cdots,m-1\}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{s,a}\bigg|b_{\mu}(s,a)-\hat{b}_{\mu}(s,a)\bigg|\leq\frac{\sqrt{p_{{\rm min}}}}{2}\cdot\epsilon,\ \

\displaystyle Y_{t}^{(1)}\leq 2\epsilon_{1}+\frac{1}{N}\cdot\frac{8}{p_{{\rm min}}}\cdot\bigg\{\sum_{k=0}^{t}\gamma^{k}\cdot\sup_{s,a}\mathbb{E}\|R(s,a)\|+\gamma^{t+1}\cdot\sup_{s,a}\mathbb{E}\|Z(s,a;\theta_{0})\|\bigg\},

\displaystyle Y_{t}^{(2)}\leq\frac{C_{3}\gamma^{t+1}}{\sqrt{N}}\cdot\sum_{s,a}\sqrt{b_{\mu}(s,a)}\cdot\bigg\{\int_{0}^{\infty}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{N}\big(\Theta,\mathbb{W}_{1,\infty},z\big)}\mathrm{d}z+u\cdot{\rm diam}\big(\Theta;\mathbb{W}_{1,\infty}\big)\bigg\},

(57)

with probability larger than the following.

\displaystyle 1

\displaystyle-2\exp(-u^{2})-(2|\mathcal{S\times A}|+6N)\cdot\exp\bigg\{\frac{-C_{5}\cdot p_{{\rm min}}\cdot N\cdot\epsilon_{1}^{2}}{d^{2}\cdot\big(\sum_{t=0}^{m-1}\gamma^{t}\big)^{2}\cdot\big(\sup_{s,a}\|R(s,a)\|_{\psi_{2}}\big)^{2}+\gamma^{2}\big(\sup_{s,a}\mathbb{E}\|Z(s,a;\theta_{0})\|\big)^{2}}\bigg\}

Before taking up Decomposition (
55
), we can further derive the following for
\forall t\in\{0,1,2,\cdots,m-1\}
, based on Inequality (
57
),

This further leads (
54
) to the following, by using
\sum_{s,a}\sqrt{b_{\mu}(s,a)}\leq 1/\sqrt{p_{{\rm min}}}
(
42
) and
m\sum_{t=0}^{m-1}\gamma^{t}\leq m^{2}
,

\displaystyle\Gamma_{N,m}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{m}{p_{{\rm min}}}\cdot\sum_{t=0}^{m-1}\gamma^{m-t-1}\cdot(Y_{t}^{(1)}+Y_{t}^{(2)})\leq E_{1}(m)\cdot\epsilon_{1}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\cdot E_{2}(m)\cdot u+\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\cdot E(m),

(58)

where

\displaystyle E_{1}(m)

\displaystyle:=\frac{2}{p_{{\rm min}}}\cdot m\sum_{t=0}^{m-1}\gamma^{t}\quad\&\quad E_{2}(m):=C_{4}\cdot m^{2}\gamma^{m}\cdot\frac{1}{p_{{\rm min}}}\sum_{s,a}\sqrt{b_{\mu}(s,a)}\cdot{\rm diam}(\Theta;\mathbb{W}_{1,\infty}),

\displaystyle E(m)

\displaystyle:=C_{4}\cdot m^{2}\cdot\frac{1}{p_{{\rm min}}^{2}}\cdot\bigg\{\gamma^{m}\cdot\int_{0}^{\infty}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{N}(\Theta,\mathbb{W}_{1,\infty},t)}\mathrm{d}t+\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\cdot\sum_{t=0}^{m-1}\gamma^{t}\cdot\sup_{s,a}\mathbb{E}\|R(s,a)\|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\cdot\gamma\cdot\sup_{s,a}\mathbb{E}\|Z(s,a;\theta_{0})\|\bigg\}.

Now let us get back to Decomposition (
55
) by further simplifying each line. Towards that end, we will first adjust the variables as follows for arbitrary
\epsilon_{0}\in(0,1]
,

\displaystyle u=\sqrt{N}\cdot p_{{\rm min}}\cdot\epsilon_{0},\ \ \&\ \ \epsilon_{1}=\sqrt{p_{{\rm min}}}\cdot\epsilon_{0},\ \ \&\ \

\displaystyle\epsilon=\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{d\cdot\sum_{t=0}^{m-1}\gamma^{t}\cdot\sup_{s,a}\|R(s,a)\|_{\psi_{2}}+\sup_{s,a}\mathbb{E}\|Z(s,a;\theta_{0})\|+1}\in(0,1]

The first line of (
55
) can be bounded as follows, based on (
58
), where
G(m):=E_{1}(m)\cdot\sqrt{p_{{\rm min}}}+E_{2}(m)\cdot p_{{\rm min}}
,

Line-1 of (
55
)

\displaystyle\leq 12\cdot\bigg\{G(m)\cdot\epsilon_{0}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\cdot E(m)\bigg\}^{1/2}\cdot\bigg[\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{\theta}^{1/2}+\bigg\{G(m)\cdot\epsilon_{0}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\cdot E(m)\bigg\}^{1/2}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq 12\cdot\bigg\{\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{\theta}^{1/2}+\sqrt{E(m)}+\sqrt{G(m)}\bigg\}\cdot\bigg\{\frac{\sqrt{E(m)}}{N^{1/4}}+\sqrt{G(m)}\cdot\sqrt{\epsilon_{0}}\bigg\},

where the last line used
\epsilon_{0}\in(0,1]
and
\sqrt{x+y}\leq\sqrt{x}+\sqrt{y}
for
x,y\geq 0
. Next we can also obtain the following,

\displaystyle\text{Line-2 of \eqref{bellman_error_decomposition}}\leq C_{7}\cdot\sqrt{p_{{\rm min}}}\cdot\bigg\{{\rm diam}(\Theta;\mathbb{W}_{1,\infty})+m\sum_{t=0}^{m-1}\gamma^{t}\cdot\bigg(1+\frac{1}{N}\cdot\frac{1}{p_{{\rm min}}}\bigg)\bigg\}\cdot\epsilon_{0}.

Switching the notation
\epsilon_{0}
into
\epsilon
, combining these eventually allows us to further rewrite Decomposition (
55
).
By using
m\sum_{t=0}^{m-1}\gamma^{t}\leq m^{2}
and (
42
), for
\forall\epsilon\in(0,1]
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\bigg|\hat{F}_{m}(\theta)-F_{m}(\theta)\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq C_{16}\cdot m^{2}\cdot\frac{1}{p_{{\rm min}}^{2}}\cdot C_{\rm env}^{(m)}(\Theta)\cdot\bigg(\frac{1}{N^{1/4}}+\sqrt{\epsilon}\bigg)

The probability bound for (
57
) can be integrated for all
t\in\{0,1,2,\cdots,m-1\}
, combined with the same trick that we used in (
40
), to obtain the following lower bound,

\displaystyle 1-C_{10}\cdot m\cdot(|\mathcal{S\times A}|+N)\cdot\exp\bigg\{-C_{11}\cdot p_{{\rm min}}^{2}\cdot N\cdot\epsilon^{2}/C_{\rm den}(m)\bigg\},

where the terms are defined as

\displaystyle C_{\rm den}(m):=d^{2}\cdot\big(\sum_{t=0}^{m-1}\gamma^{t}\big)^{2}\cdot\big(\sup_{s,a}\|R(s,a)\|_{\psi_{2}}\big)^{2}+\gamma^{2}\cdot\big(\sup_{s,a}\mathbb{E}\|Z(s,a;\theta_{0})\|\big)^{2}+1,

(59)

\displaystyle C_{\rm env}^{(m)}(\Theta):=L\cdot(\gamma^{m}\sqrt{p}+1)\cdot{\rm diam}(\Theta;\|\cdot\|)+\sum_{t=0}^{m-1}\gamma^{t}\cdot\sup_{s,a}\mathbb{E}\|R(s,a)\|+(1+\gamma^{m})\cdot\sup_{s,a}\mathbb{E}\|Z(s,a;\theta_{0})\|+1.

This gives us the desired result of Lemma
B.1
.

B.5
Obtaining The Bound Of Bootstrap-based Objective Function

Our final estimator of the objective function
\hat{F}_{m}^{(B)}
(
18
) is based on bootstrap, not
\hat{F}_{m}
(
18
) covered in Lemma
B.1
. So we shall develop it into following.

Lemma B.3
.

Under same assumptions of Lemma
B.1
, for any
m\in\mathbb{N}
and arbitrary
\epsilon,\epsilon^{\prime}\in(0,1]
,

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\bigg|\hat{F}_{m}^{(B)}(\theta)-F_{m}(\theta)\bigg|\leq\frac{C_{32}}{p_{{\rm min}}^{2}}\cdot B_{\rm env}^{(m)}(\Theta)\cdot\bigg\{m^{2}\cdot\bigg(\frac{1}{N^{1/4}}+\sqrt{\epsilon}\bigg)+m\cdot\bigg(\frac{1}{M^{1/4}}+\sqrt{\epsilon^{\prime}}\bigg)\bigg\}

holds with probability larger than

\displaystyle 1-\mathcal{D}(N)-C_{1}\cdot m\cdot(|\mathcal{S\times A}|+N)\cdot\exp(-C_{2}\cdot p_{{\rm min}}^{2}\cdot N\cdot\epsilon^{2}/C_{\rm den}(m))

\displaystyle\ \ \ -C_{1}\cdot(|\mathcal{S\times A}|+M)\cdot\exp(-C_{2}\cdot p_{{\rm min}}^{2}\cdot M\cdot{\epsilon^{\prime}}^{2}/B_{\rm den}(m)),

where
C_{\rm den}(m)
,
B_{\rm den}(m)
,
B_{\rm env}^{(m)}(\Theta)
are defined in (
59
), (
66
), and
\mathcal{D}(N)\rightarrow 0
as in (
76
).

B.5.1
Three Stages Of Probability Space

We can decompose the term
\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}|\hat{F}_{m}^{(B)}(\theta)-F_{m}(\theta)|
as follows,

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\bigg|\hat{F}_{m}^{(B)}(\theta)-F_{m}(\theta)\bigg|\leq\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\bigg|\hat{F}_{m}^{(B)}(\theta)-\hat{F}_{m}(\theta)\bigg|+\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\bigg|\hat{F}_{m}(\theta)-F_{m}(\theta)\bigg|.

(60)

At this point, we should recognize that our probability space (
28
) is expanded due to bootstrapping procedure reflected in
\hat{F}_{m}^{(B)}
. Now our probability space
(\Omega,\Sigma,\mathbb{P})
can be factorized into three stages,

Stage 1:

\displaystyle\ (\Omega_{\mathcal{S\times A}},\Sigma_{\mathcal{S\times A}},\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{S\times A}})\Rightarrow\text{determines which state-action pairs }S_{i},A_{i}\text{ are sampled,}

(61)

Stage 2:

\displaystyle\ (\Omega^{(\mathbf{N})},\Sigma^{(\mathbf{N})},\mathbb{P}^{(\mathbf{N})})\Rightarrow\text{conditioned on }(S_{i},A_{i}),\text{ determines }R_{i},S_{i}^{\prime}\sim p(\cdots|S_{i},A_{i}),

Stage 3:

\displaystyle\ (\Omega_{B}^{(\mathcal{D})},\Sigma_{B}^{(\mathcal{D})},\mathbb{P}_{B}^{(\mathcal{D})})\Rightarrow\text{conditioned on }\mathcal{D},\text{ determines the bootstrapped trajectories in \eqref{Bootstrap_operator}}.

We have already bounded
\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}|\hat{F}_{m}(\theta)-F_{m}(\theta)|
of (
60
) in Lemma
B.1
, which is controlled by Stage 1 and 2 probability spaces (
61
). Now the remaining term
\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}|\hat{F}_{m}^{(B)}(\theta)-\hat{F}_{m}(\theta)|
of (
60
) is solely based on Stage 3 probability space (
61
), conditioned on the observed data
\mathcal{D}=\{(s_{i},a_{i},r_{i},s_{i}^{\prime})\}_{i=1}^{N}
. However, since the bootstrapped probability space (Stage 3) is affected by what was observed in the previous two stages, we will assume some nice properties are satisfied in Stage 1 and Stage 2 probability spaces, which are already mentioned within the proof of Lemma
B.1
in
B.4
.

B.5.2
Inherited Results From Lemma
B.1

Here we will define two events. The first event
E_{1,a}
is equivalent event to
\Omega_{\mathcal{S\times A}}^{(\epsilon)}
(
29
), under which Facts (
30
) are satisfied.
Next, we will define the second event
E_{1,b}
where the following two statements are satisfied, which we inherit from Lemma
B.1
,

\displaystyle\Gamma_{N,m}\leq E_{1}(m)\cdot\sqrt{p_{{\rm min}}}\cdot\epsilon+E_{2}(m)\cdot p_{{\rm min}}\cdot\epsilon+\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\cdot E(m),

(62)

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\bigg|\hat{F}_{m}(\theta)-F_{m}(\theta)\bigg|\leq C_{1}\cdot m^{2}\cdot\frac{1}{p_{{\rm min}}^{2}}\cdot C_{\rm env}^{(m)}(\Theta)\cdot\bigg(\frac{1}{N^{1/4}}+\sqrt{\epsilon}\bigg)

We have derived in the last part of Appendix
B.4.2
that both events hold with probability larger than
1-C_{2}\cdot m\cdot(|\mathcal{S\times A}|+N)\cdot\exp\big\{-C_{3}\cdot p_{{\rm min}}^{2}\cdot N\cdot\epsilon^{2}/C_{\rm den}(m)\big\}
.

Let us assume that the events
E_{1,a}
and
E_{1,b}
both hold, and then bound the term
\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}|\hat{F}_{m}^{(B)}(\theta)-\hat{F}_{m}(\theta)|
of (
60
). We need to emphasize that at this moment,
\mathcal{D}
is given, and Stage 3 probability space (
61
) is the only source of probability. In other words, we can consider
\hat{F}_{m}
as our population objective function, which is based upon
\hat{b}_{\mu}
(
9
) and
\hat{p}_{m}(\cdots|s,a)
.
\hat{p}_{m}(\cdots|s,a)
represents the empirical measure of
(\sum_{t=0}^{m-1}\gamma^{t}\hat{R}^{(t)},\hat{S}^{(m)})
conditioned on initial state-action pair
s,a
that can occur by applying
\hat{p}(r,s^{\prime}|s,a)
and
\pi(a|s)
for
m
consecutive times (
50
). In other words, by treating
(\hat{\mathcal{T}}^{\pi})^{m}
as the population operator and
\mathcal{B}_{m}
as its approximation, we can obtain

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\bigg|\hat{F}_{m}^{(B)}(\theta)-\hat{F}_{m}(\theta)\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq 12\cdot\tilde{\Gamma}_{B,m}^{1/2}\cdot\bigg\{\tilde{\Gamma}_{B,m}^{1/2}+\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\hat{\bar{\mathcal{E}}}\big\{\Upsilon_{\theta},(\hat{\mathcal{T}}^{\pi})^{m}\Upsilon_{\theta}\big\}^{1/2}\bigg\}\ \ \ \text{by same logic in Appendix \ref{Proof_Fmhat_convergence}},

\displaystyle\leq C_{4}\cdot\tilde{\Gamma}_{B,m}^{1/2}\cdot\bigg\{\tilde{\Gamma}_{B,m}^{1/2}+\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{\theta}^{1/2}+\Gamma_{N,m}^{1/2}\bigg\}.

(63)

where we have a new term that we will refer to as
bootstrap discrepancy

\displaystyle\tilde{\Gamma}_{B,m}:=\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\hat{\bar{\mathcal{E}}}\big\{(\hat{\mathcal{T}}^{\pi})^{m}\Upsilon_{\theta},\mathcal{B}_{m}\Upsilon_{\theta}\big\}.

Let us bound the three terms one by one. First, we can bound the supremum term
\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{\theta}^{1/2}
by following, based on Assumption
4.1
,

\displaystyle 2\cdot\bigg\{\sqrt{1+\gamma^{m}}\cdot\sqrt{L}\cdot\sqrt{{\rm diam}(\Theta;\|\cdot\|)}+\bigg(\sum_{t=0}^{m-1}\gamma^{t}\cdot\sup_{s,a}\mathbb{E}\|R(s,a)\|\bigg)^{1/2}+\bigg((1+\gamma^{m})\cdot\sup_{s,a}\mathbb{E}\|Z(s,a;\theta_{0})\|\bigg)^{1/2}\bigg\}

Based on what we have mentioned in the beginning of
B.5.2
, we can further bound Bellman discrepancy as follows by using (
62
),

\displaystyle\Gamma_{N,m}^{1/2}\leq C_{8}\cdot\frac{m}{p_{{\rm min}}^{1/4}}\cdot\bigg\{1+\gamma^{m/2}\cdot\sqrt{L}\cdot\sqrt{{\rm diam}(\Theta;\|\cdot\|)}\bigg\}\cdot\sqrt{\epsilon}+\frac{C_{8}}{N^{1/4}}\cdot\frac{m}{p_{{\rm min}}}\cdot\sqrt{L}\cdot p^{1/4}\cdot\gamma^{m/2}\cdot\sqrt{{\rm diam}(\Theta;\|\cdot\|)}

\displaystyle+\frac{C_{8}}{\sqrt{N}}\cdot\frac{m}{p_{{\rm min}}}\cdot\bigg\{\bigg(\sum_{t=0}^{m-1}\gamma^{t}\cdot\sup_{s,a}\mathbb{E}\|R(s,a)\|\bigg)^{1/2}+\bigg(\gamma\cdot\sup_{s,a}\mathbb{E}\|Z(s,a;\theta_{0})\|\bigg)^{1/2}\bigg\},

where the second last inequality can be derived by putting together Assumption
4.1
, Inequality (
42
), and Remark
A.5
. Since both of these hold under
E_{1,a}
and
E_{1,b}
, the probability is not reduced.

B.5.3
Bounding Bootstrap Discrepancy

In further bounding (
63
), bootstrap discrepancy
\tilde{\Gamma}_{B,m}
is the only term is probabilistic due to Stage 3 probability space (
61
). Comparing (
50
) and (
51
), we can see that
\mathcal{B}_{m}
is in fact the single-step estimator of
(\hat{\mathcal{T}}^{\pi})^{m}
that can be viewed as a new population operator in the new probability space generated by bootstrapping from the already-observed data
\mathcal{D}
. In this regard, the relationship between
\mathcal{B}_{m}
and
(\hat{\mathcal{T}}^{\pi})^{m}
aligns with that between
\hat{\mathcal{T}}^{\pi}
and
\mathcal{T}^{\pi}
, only with a few differences. The reward
R
is replaced by discounted sum
\sum_{t=1}^{m}\gamma^{t-1}\hat{R}^{(t)}
,
S^{\prime}
is replaced by
\hat{S}^{(m)}
,
A^{\prime}
is replaced by
\hat{A}^{(m)}
, and the discount rate
\gamma
is replaced by
\gamma^{m}
. In addition, several other quantities are also replaced as follows,

\displaystyle b_{\mu}(s,a)\leftarrow\hat{b}_{\mu}(s,a),\ \ \ \mathbb{E}(\cdots)\leftarrow\tilde{\mathbb{E}}(\cdots),\ \ \ \|\cdot\|_{\psi_{2}}\leftarrow\|\cdot\|_{\tilde{\psi}_{2}},\ \ \ N\leftarrow M,

\displaystyle p_{{\rm min}}\leftarrow\hat{p}_{\rm min}:=\min\{\hat{b}_{\mu}(s,a)\ :\ \hat{b}_{\mu}(s,a)>0\}=\min\{\hat{b}_{\mu}(s,a)\}\ \ \text{by Facts (\ref{Fact1to3})}.

where
\tilde{\mathbb{E}}(\cdots)
(
11
) and
\|\cdot\|_{\tilde{\psi}_{2}}
are the expectation and sub-Gaussian norms corresponding to the conditional probability measure
\mathbb{P}(\cdots|\mathcal{D})
. With the replacements by the estimated quantities (that will now be regarded as a new population quantity in Stage 3 probability space), we can replicate the proofs of
A.7.3
. Skipping the calculation details, we obtain the following with
an arbitrary
\epsilon^{\prime}\in(0,1]
,

\displaystyle\tilde{\Gamma}_{B,m}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{M}\cdot\frac{8}{\hat{p}_{\rm min}}\cdot\bigg(\sum_{t=1}^{m}\gamma^{t-1}\cdot\sup_{s,a}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\|\hat{R}(s,a)\|+\gamma^{m}\cdot\sup_{s,a}\mathbb{E}\|Z(s,a;\theta_{0})\|\bigg)

(64)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{C_{13}}{\sqrt{M}}\cdot\gamma^{m}\cdot\sum_{s,a}\sqrt{\hat{b}_{\mu}(s,a)}\cdot L\sqrt{p}\cdot{\rm diam}(\Theta;\|\cdot\|)+C_{14}\cdot(1+\gamma^{m})\cdot\sqrt{\hat{p}_{\rm min}}\cdot\epsilon^{\prime},

with probability larger than

\displaystyle 1-C_{15}\cdot(|\mathcal{S\times A}|+M)\times\exp\bigg\{\frac{-C_{16}\cdot\hat{p}_{\rm min}^{2}\cdot M\cdot{\epsilon^{\prime}}^{2}}{\big(\sum_{t=1}^{m}\gamma^{t-1}\cdot\sup_{s,a}\big\|\|\hat{R}^{(t)}(s,a)\|\big\|_{\tilde{\psi}_{2}}+\gamma^{m}\cdot\sup_{s,a}\mathbb{E}\|Z(s,a;\theta_{0})\|+1\big)^{2}}\bigg\}.

Note that this is probability bound conditioned under
E_{1,a}\cap E_{1,b}
.

B.5.4
Finalizing The Bound

The bound (
64
) are not yet useful though, since they are not fully represented with population quantities. This is because we are caring about Stage 3 probability space (
61
) conditioned upon the observed data
\mathcal{D}
(that is associated with Stage 1 and 2 probability spaces). So we hope to bound the following terms with the corresponding population quantities,

\displaystyle\sum_{s,a}\sqrt{\hat{b}_{\mu}(s,a)}\ \ \&\ \ \hat{p}_{\rm min}\ \ \&\ \ \sup_{s,a}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\|\hat{R}(s,a)\|\ \ \&\ \ \sup_{s,a}\big\|\|\hat{R}(s,a)\|\big\|_{\tilde{\psi}_{2}},

(65)

but it comes with a price, that is subtraction of probability.

Let us first condition upon
E_{1,a}
. It is straightforward to derive
\sum_{s,a}\sqrt{\hat{b}_{\mu}(s,a)}\leq\sqrt{\frac{3}{2}}\cdot\sum_{s,a}\sqrt{b_{\mu}(s,a)}
and
\frac{1}{2}p_{{\rm min}}\leq\hat{p}_{\rm min}\leq\frac{3}{2}p_{{\rm min}}
.
Unlike the first two terms of (
65
) that could be bounded solely
E_{1,a}
, the remaining two terms cannot be deterministically bounded, necessitating the derivation of probabilistic bound.

Since we are conditioning on
E_{1,a}
, we should deal with Stage 2 probability space (
61
), using the conditional probability
\mathbb{P}^{(\mathbf{N})}(\cdots)
introduced below Definition (
29
). Based on Derivation (
38
) for an arbitrary
\epsilon_{3}^{\prime}\in(0,1]
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{(\mathbf{N})}\bigg[\sup_{s,a}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\|\hat{R}(s,a)\|\leq\sup_{s,a}\mathbb{E}\|R(s,a)\|+\sqrt{p_{{\rm min}}}\cdot\epsilon_{3}^{\prime}\bigg]\geq 1-2|\mathcal{S\times A}|\cdot\exp\bigg\{\frac{-C_{17}\cdot p_{{\rm min}}^{2}\cdot N\cdot{\epsilon_{3}^{\prime}}^{2}}{d^{2}\cdot\big(\sup_{s,a}\|R(s,a)\|_{\psi_{2}}\big)^{2}}\bigg\},

Now let us bound the forth term of (
65
). First, let
s,a\in\mathcal{S\times A}
be arbitrary. Define two random variables
U(s,a):=\|R(s,a)\|
and
\hat{U}(s,a):=\|\hat{R}(s,a)\|
, along with the following functions,

\displaystyle A_{s,a}(t):=\mathbb{E}\bigg\{\exp\bigg(\frac{U(s,a)^{2}}{t^{2}}\bigg)\bigg\},\quad\hat{A}_{s,a}(t):=\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\bigg\{\exp\bigg(\frac{\hat{U}(s,a)^{2}}{t^{2}}\bigg)\bigg\}=\frac{1}{N(s,a)}\sum_{i=1}^{N(s,a)}\exp\bigg(\frac{U_{i}(s,a)^{2}}{t^{2}}\bigg),

where
U_{i}(s,a)=\|R_{i}(s,a)\|\ (1\leq i\leq N(s,a))
represents the samples. Let
t_{0}(s,a)>0
be the value such that
A_{s,a}\big(t_{0}(s,a)\big)=1
.
It is obvious to see
t_{0}(s,a)>\|\|R(s,a)\|\|_{\psi_{2}}
, based on that
A_{s,a}(\|\|R(s,a)\|\|_{\psi_{2}})=2
holds and
A_{s,a}(t)
is a strictly decreasing function. We can bound its probability term as follows,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{(\mathbf{N})}\big(\big\|\|\hat{R}(s,a)\|

\displaystyle\big\|_{\tilde{\psi}_{2}}\leq t_{0}(s,a)\big)=\mathbb{P}^{(\mathbf{N})}\big\{\hat{A}_{s,a}\big(t_{0}(s,a)\big)\leq 2\big\}\ \ \ \text{by Definition (\ref{subgaussian_norm_definition})}

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}^{(\mathbf{N})}\bigg\{\bigg|\hat{A}_{s,a}\big(t_{0}(s,a)\big)-A_{s,a}\big(t_{0}(s,a)\big)\bigg|\leq 2-A_{s,a}\big(t_{0}(s,a)\big)\bigg\}

\displaystyle\geq 1-\mathbb{E}^{(\mathbf{N})}\big|\hat{A}_{s,a}\big(t_{0}(s,a)\big)-A_{s,a}\big(t_{0}(s,a)\big)\big|\ \ \ \text{by Markov's Inequality}.

Note that we could apply Markov’s Inequality in the last line since
\mathbb{E}|A_{s,a}(t)|<\infty
by sub-Gaussianity assumption
3.2
that implies
\|U(s,a)\|=\|\|R(s,a)\|\|_{\psi_{2}}<\infty
.
Now let us shrink the above term
with the following lemma that is proved in
C.1.8
,

Lemma B.4
.

If
X_{i}\ (1\leq i\leq n)
are iid with
\mathbb{E}(X_{1})=0
,
\mathbb{E}|X_{1}|<\infty
, then the expectation of the sample mean shrinks to zero as follows,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big|\bar{X}_{n}\big|\leq\inf_{z>0}\bigg[\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\cdot\bigg\{\mathbb{E}\big\{X_{1}^{2}\cdot\mathbf{1}(|X_{1}|\leq z)\big\}\bigg\}^{1/2}+\mathbb{E}\big\{|X_{1}|\cdot\mathbf{1}(|X_{1}|>z)\big\}\bigg]\rightarrow 0\ \ \ \text{as }n\rightarrow\infty.

Note that we have a deterministic sequence its convergence to zero is guaranteed, however its speed depends on the tail of the distribution
X_{1}
.

By defining
V(s,a):=\exp(U(s,a)^{2}/t_{0}(s,a)^{2})
, we can apply Lemma
B.4
to bound
\mathbb{E}^{(\mathbf{N})}|\hat{A}_{s,a}(t_{0}(s,a))-A_{s,a}(t_{0}(s,a))|
by

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{2}{p_{{\rm min}}}}\cdot\inf_{z>0}\bigg[\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\cdot\bigg\{\mathbb{E}\big\{V(s,a)^{2}\cdot\mathbf{1}\big(V(s,a)\leq z\big)\big\}\bigg\}^{1/2}+\mathbb{E}\big\{V(s,a)\cdot\mathbf{1}\big(V(s,a)>z\big)\big\}\bigg],

where the last line holds by Facts (
30
). Now defining the following new variable
t_{0}^{*}:=\sup_{s,a}t_{0}(s,a)
which implies
A_{s,a}(t_{0}^{*})\leq 1
for all
s,a\in\mathcal{S\times A}
,
we have the following (see (
76
) for definition of
\mathcal{D}(N)
),

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{(\mathbf{N})}\bigg\{\sup_{s,a}\big\|\|\hat{R}(s,a)\|\big\|_{\tilde{\psi}_{2}}\leq t_{0}^{*}\bigg\}\

\displaystyle\geq 1-\mathcal{D}(N),

By letting
\epsilon_{3}^{\prime}=\epsilon^{\prime}\in(0,1]
, we can bound all four estimated quantities (
65
) at the same time as follows,

\displaystyle\sup_{s,a}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\|\hat{R}(s,a)\|\leq\sup_{s,a}\mathbb{E}\|R(s,a)\|+\epsilon^{\prime}\quad\&\quad\sup_{s,a}\big\|\|\hat{R}(s,a)\|\big\|_{\tilde{\psi}_{2}}\leq t_{0}^{*},

with probability larger than

\displaystyle 1-2|\mathcal{S\times A}|\cdot\exp\bigg\{\frac{-C_{17}\cdot p_{{\rm min}}^{2}\cdot N\cdot{\epsilon^{\prime}}^{2}}{d^{2}\cdot\big(\sup_{s,a}\|R(s,a)\|_{\psi_{2}}\big)^{2}}\bigg\}-\mathcal{D}(N).

Let us define a new event called
E_{2}
where all these four quantities (
65
) are bounded as we mentioned.

Now that we have bounded the estimated quantities with its population counterparts, we can rewrite the bound of
\tilde{\Gamma}_{B,m}
(
64
) accordingly, under
E_{1,a},E_{1,b},E_{2}
. Then, skipping the technical details, we can aggregate all aforementioned results to obtain

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\bigg|\hat{F}_{m}^{(B)}(\theta)-\hat{F}_{m}(\theta)\bigg|\leq\frac{C_{31}}{p_{{\rm min}}^{3/2}}\cdot m\cdot B_{\rm env}^{(m)}(\Theta)\cdot\bigg(\frac{1}{M^{1/4}}+\sqrt{\epsilon^{\prime}}\bigg)

(66)

\displaystyle\text{where}\quad B_{\rm env}^{(m)}(\Theta):=L\sqrt{p}\cdot{\rm diam}(\Theta;\|\cdot\|)+\sum_{t=0}^{m-1}\gamma^{t}\cdot\sup_{s,a}\mathbb{E}\|R(s,a)\|+\sup_{s,a}\mathbb{E}\|Z(s,a;\theta_{0})\|+1,

\displaystyle\text{and}\quad B_{\rm den}(m):=\big(\sum_{t=0}^{m-1}\gamma^{t}\big)^{2}\cdot{t_{0}^{*}}^{2}+\gamma^{2m}\cdot\big(\sup_{s,a}\mathbb{E}\|Z(s,a;\theta_{0})\|\big)^{2}+1,

with probability larger than

\displaystyle 1-\mathcal{D}(N)-C_{22}\cdot m\cdot(|\mathcal{S\times A}|+N)\cdot\exp(-C_{23}\cdot p_{{\rm min}}^{2}\cdot N\cdot\epsilon^{2}/C_{\rm den}(m))

\displaystyle\quad-C_{22}\cdot(|\mathcal{S\times A}|+M)\cdot\exp(-C_{23}\cdot p_{{\rm min}}^{2}\cdot M\cdot{\epsilon^{\prime}}^{2}/B_{\rm den}(m)).

Now the final task is to incorporate this with the bound of Lemma
B.1
, based on Decomposition (
60
). As we mentioned in the beginning of
B.5.2
, we already inherited the bound, so we do not have to subtract any additional probability from the current bound (
66
). Then we have

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\bigg|\hat{F}_{m}^{(B)}(\theta)-F_{m}(\theta)\bigg|
